STRUCTURAL (2026-11-20): arm9 .text is 227,048 B across 233 gaps; only 42,040 B is delinked (797 entries). 185,008 B (81%) sits in gap regions filled from the original binary. ready.py lists symbol-table candidates, and reg.py splits a gap on install, so gap targets are normal - BUT some gap symbol sizes are heuristic and wrong (0203aeb8 '12B' branches past its own extent; 02033c58 starts mid-function; 02032b00 is a fragment). Those are unmatchable until symbols.txt is cleaned up. That cleanup is a separate job, not a matching task.





FILTER RESULT (2026-11-20): of arm9's ready list, only 4 units are free of ldm/stm/mul/mla/blx/pre-indexed addressing. All four hit walls: 02015524 (52B, add r2,r1,#0xc materialisation, 48B), 02032c38 (56B, retail reads bytes via aligned ldrh+mask, mwcc emits ldrb, 40B), 020341f4 (56B, add ip,r0,#0x28 materialisation, 52B), 0201fd50 (60B, retail pools all three bases and interleaves them, mwcc reloads later, 76B). The easy arm9 pool is exhausted for source-shape work.

020208b0 / 0202093c 140B+140B arm9 (famsurvey group) -- under 1.2/b56 the two best candidates





  (`build/cand/020208b0_c.c`, `_d.c`) hit 140/140 with a three-register rotation left: retail has





  `mvn ip, r0` and the flags word in r2, we get `mvn r3, r0` and the flags in ip. Everything else in





  the body lines up. sp2 note follows. -- 140/140 each, 6-7 diff, purely a register swap: retail holds the inverted argument in ip and the flags word in r3 (`mvn ip,r0` / `ldrh r3,[r1]` / `orr r2,r3,r2` / `and r2,ip,r2`), mwcc always puts the mask in r3 and the flags word in ip. Body proven: read old=(u16*)(g+0x18), fl=(u16*)g, store a0 back, `g[0] = (fl|old) & ~a0`, then `if (a0==0) goto zero; if (a0==0x100) { HW |= mask; *(u8*)0x04000249 = 0x83; } goto out; zero: HW &= ~mask; out: func_02021538(g[0]);` where the call is SHARED after the branch - duplicating it costs 4 B and if-converting the zero arm costs 4 B. goto form and the nested if/else form both give 140 B. Tried: mask expression order, load order, explicit u32/u16 mask variable assigned before/after loads and after the store, base pointer with p[0xc]/p[0], separate pointer to +0x18 - all land on the same swapped pair.





02017954 64B arm9 -- **1.2/b56 gets the frame and lands on 64/64**; the only residue is a register





  tie (retail keeps `off` in ip, zero in r3, the count address in r2; b56 gives off in r3, zero in r2).





  Tried under b56: declaration order both ways, u32 off, u32 *t pointer, local zero variable, reversed





  store order (48B), off via struct. Original sp2 note follows. 44/64: body recovered (n = *(u16 *)(p+0xa); t = q + n; zero 8 bytes at t; p[0]=p[1]=q; *(u16 *)(p+8) += 1) but retail carries a NON-LEAF FRAME (`stmdb sp!,{lr}` + `sub sp,sp,#4` + matching teardown) and 5 extra instructions (separate `add lr` for one of the two zero stores, zero in r3, count address in r2) although it contains no call and never touches [sp]. Every spelling tried (u8* p+off, struct fields, u32* t, local zero variable, reversed store order) gives 44 B with no frame. The unused 4-byte stack slot suggests an elided local or a by-value struct parameter in the original; not reachable from the visible body.





0201779c 112B arm9 -- 104/112 (22 diff): free-counterpart of 02017954, body correct (n = *(u16 *)(p+0xa); t = q + n; if (t->a == 0) p->head = t->b else *(u32 *)(t->a + n + 4) = t->b; mirror for t->b with *(u32 *)(t->b + n) = t->a; clear t->a/t->b; *(u16 *)(p+8) -= 1). Remaining 8 B is the SAME unexplained 8-byte-aligned frame as its sibling 02017954 (`stmdb sp!,{lr}` + `sub sp,sp,#4` + teardown with no call and no [sp] access). Both functions in this family carry it; 8-byte alignment points at a u64/double-aligned local or a `__packed` struct temp in the original that the visible body does not use. Body alone compiles smaller with no frame and only register-name diffs, so the logic is right and the frame is the whole blocker.











## ov002 0204d488 (80B, 64/80)





retail splits the counter address two ways: read path `add r0,r2,#0x30000; ldrh r1,[r0,#0x48]`,





RMW path `ldrlo r0,=0x30048; addlo r1,r2,r0; ldrhlo r0,[r1]; addlo r0,#1; strhlo r0,[r1]`.





mwcc folds both to `[r0,#0x48]` for every spelling tried (3). Needs the pool-materialised RMW that





this compiler will not emit for a constant that also fits add+offset.











## ov002 020633e0 (84B, 76/84)





retail materialises `add r2,r1,#0x24` for the paired ORR/BIC RMW; mwcc folds to [rBase,#0x24]. Same





fold/materialise tie as arm9 0201ce4c. Bound-pointer and direct spellings both fold.











## ov002 0200fbc0 / 0200fc18 (64B each, 60/64)





retail keeps the pointer chain in r3 and loads the value into r3 as well, so the field address has to





live in ip (`add ip,r3,#2`). mwcc puts the pointer in ip and the value in r3, then folds. Mirror





register allocation, not a source shape - the same tie seen in 0204d488.











## ov002 02084e94 (220B)





Jump-table switch (`addls pc,pc,r5,lsl #2`), logic fully decoded and size-exact at 220B, but every





spelling lands with the register set rotated one slot: retail keeps the loop counter in r3, the





clear-flag zero in r0 and the store constant in r4 (`mov r3,#0` / `mov r4,r3` / `mov r0,r3` /





`mov r6,#1`), mwcc insists on r4 for the counter (`mov r4,#0` / `mov r5,r4` / `mov r1,r4`).





Tried: 7 declaration orders, do-while vs for, u8/u32/s32 flag, `if (clr)` vs `if (clr == 1)`.





Declaring the loop counter `s32` is required for `blt` (not `blo`) - that part is settled.











## ov002 020780f8 (188B, 180/188)





Bisection switch decoded exactly (cases 0x14/0x17/0x18/0x13d/0x13e set bit 2, default clears it).





Blocks line up; the 8-byte gap is how mwcc materialises `base + 0x1e6c` and `base + 0x1824`.





Retail pool-loads both (`ldr r0,[pc]; add r0,ip,r0`); mwcc splits 0x1e6c as `add r1,ip,#0x1000` +





`[r1,#0xe6c]`. Tried 5 spellings for the two pointers (`base + K`, `&base[K]`, `p += K`, cast adds,





u8/u32 base) - all split, none pool-load.











## ov003 02066e84 family (196B x4: 02066e84 020b8904 020b8e54 020dbad4)





Decoded completely: DISPCNT-style mask write, one call to func_02021b14 with 5 args, two conditional





u16 counters at 0x897e (inc if <0x10) and 0x8986 (dec if !=0), then a 0x04001000 mask write.





`| 0x1f | 0x20` must be two separate statements or it folds to `orr #0x3f`.





Size reaches 196B with the pooled-address lever (see notes 2.10), but the address pointer lands in `ip`





where retail uses `r3`, and the pool literal count still differs. Budget one more session with a





register-pressure experiment; the logic is not in doubt.











## ov002 02063820 / 02063c5c (84B, best 84/84 with 5 diffs)





Shape: index a 0x38-stride table, test a bit in [p+0x24], then ORR one bit / BIC another through the





same field. `u32 *q = (u32 *)(p + 0x24); *q |= A; *q &= ~B;` reproduces retail's materialised





`add r2, r1, #0x24` and the double `[r2]` access - size lands exactly on 84B. The only residue: mwcc





hoists the `add` above the early return, retail places it after `bxeq lr`. Tried 8 orderings





(q before/after the test, if/else with a result variable, goto, nested inverted conditions, typed vs





byte pointer). Same one-instruction hoist as arm9 0200f9a4, so it is a scheduler behaviour, not a shape.











## ov002 020e4450 (84B, best 84/84 with 1 diff)





`p = (u32 *)(base + 0x54 + a0 * 4); *p &= ~0x30;` then a1==0 returns, a1==1 sets bit 0x10, a1==2 sets





bit 0x20. The nested-inversion form `if (a1 != 1) { if (a1 == 2) {...} } *p |= 0x10;` lands on exactly





84B with retail's predicated `ldreq/orreq/streq` block. Residue: retail ends that block with an





unconditional `bx lr`, mwcc emits `bxeq lr`. Tried 9 shapes (goto, switch, duplicated tails, explicit





`if (a1 != 1) return;`). Same predicated-return residue as several arm9 walls.











## ov002 020f2970 (84B, best 84/84 with 9 diffs)





Per-slot reset: reads [p+0xd1], writes 0 to base+a0+0x142 (or +0x14a when the byte is 2) and copies the





byte at +0x146/+0x14e into [p+0xac]. Everything matches - predication polarity, block order, size 84B -





with `if (v == 2) {142 block} else {14a block}`. Sole residue: retail keeps `base + a0` in r0, mwcc in





r1 (the zero constant and the load follow the swap). Tried inline expressions, separate variables and





three declaration orders. Pure allocation tie.

FAMILY ov003 4x196 (0x2066e84 / 0xb8904 / 0xb8e54 / 0xdbad4): 196B. Body 40 bytes SHORT of retail





  (pool at #0x74 where retail has #0x9c). Source derived and confirmed against retail literals:





    v = *(volatile u16*)0x04001048; v = (v & ~0x3f) | 0x1f; v = v | 0x20; *(volatile u16*)0x04001048 = v;





    func_02021b14(0x04001050, 0xe, 0x24, *(u16*)(b+0x897e), *(u16*)(b+0x8986));





    if (*(u16*)(b+0x897e) < 0x10) (*(u16*)(b+0x897e))++;

    *(volatile u32*)0x04001000 |= 0x1500;





  Missing 8-10 instructions. Two facts established by reading retail's tail directly:





  (1) retail does NOT keep a base pointer: for the big offsets it loads the constant from the pool and





      does `ldrlo r0,[pc] ; addlo r1,r4,r0` (0x897e pooled, add) - a base pointer makes mwcc fold to





      `ldrh rX,[r4,#0x7e]` and lose those two instructions per access.

      `bic r1,r1,#0x1f00` + `orr r1,r1,#0x1500` (clear-then-set), with a stray `mov r0,#0` in between.





      Writing `v = (v & ~0x1f00) | 0x1500` as two statements still does not reproduce the schedule.





  Both formulations tried (q base pointer + OR, direct expression + bic/orr): 148 and 164 of 196 bytes.





  PARKED at 5 attempts.











FAMILY arm9 2x96 (0x2023df8 / 0x2023e58): identical bar the base constant (0x06600000 / 0x06400000).





  Retail: `push {r4,r5,lr}`, `mvn ip,#0`, `mov r5,r0`, `mov r4,r1`, `mov r3,r2` (r3 DEAD),





  `cmp lr,ip`, `beq else`, then func_020268e0(h, a0, base+a1) / func_020269ec(a0, base+a1).

  `mov r3,r2` is the tell: the source's 3rd parameter is used in a way we have not found.

NOTE (new class): when the *whole* listing is offset by exactly one instruction, the residue is a





  frame difference, not an instruction difference. Compare frames first: retail's extra `sub sp,#4`





  means the source had one more address-taken 4-byte local than the reconstruction.











ADDENDUM 4x196 (0x2066e84 family), from reading retail's tail directly:





  (1) retail does NOT keep a base pointer. For the big offsets it loads the constant from the pool and





      does `ldrlo r0,[pc] ; addlo r1,r4,r0` (0x897e pooled + add). Keeping a `u16 *q` makes mwcc fold to





      `ldrh rX,[r4,#0x7e]` and silently loses 2 instructions per access.





  (2) the 0x04001000 tail is NOT `|= 0x1500`. Retail loads the word early into r2, then





      `bic r1,r1,#0x1f00` + `orr r1,r1,#0x1500` (clear-then-set), with a stray `mov r0,#0` between them.





      Writing `v = (v & ~0x1f00) | 0x1500` as two statements still does not reproduce retail's schedule.





  Both formulations tried (q pointer + OR: 148/196 bytes; direct expression + bic/orr: 164/196). PARKED.

=== BIOS SWI stubs: object MATCH, ROM rejects (gap blob, see notes 2.45) ===





Shape: '#include "types.h"' + blank + '#pragma thumb on' + fn + '{ asm { swi N } }'. All need reg.py = False.





  arm9 0x020040e6  4B Halt                                asm { swi 0x06 }





  arm9 0x02004146  4B Sqrt                                asm { swi 0x0d }





  arm9 0x0200426a  4B RLUnCompReadNormalWrite8bit         asm { swi 0x14 }





  arm9 0x02004336  4B IsDebugger                          asm { swi 0x0f }





  arm9 0x0200440e  4B LZ77UnCompReadByCallbackWrite16bit  asm { swi 0x12 }





  arm9 0x02004466  4B CpuSet                              asm { swi 0x0b }





  arm9 0x020044ea  4B GetCRC16                            asm { swi 0x0e }





  arm9 0x0200470a  4B CpuFastSet                          asm { swi 0x0c }





  arm9 0x020042d2  6B VBlankIntrWait   asm { mov r2, #0; swi 0x05 }





  arm9 0x020043a8  6B Mod             asm { swi 0x09; mov r0, r1 }

RESOLVED 2026-11-24: all ten of these are matched. They were never codegen
walls; they were blocked by section placement, which `tools/align_code.py` now
fixes in the build (see below). The note is kept because the same mechanism
covers any future function whose start is 4n+2.

* `#pragma thumb on` + `asm { swi N }` *does* byte-match in isolation. `Halt`
  (4 B) and `Mod` (6 B) both report `MATCH` from `fastmatch diffc`.
* They still miss at link level because of halfword placement. `Mod` at
  0x020043a8 links its own six bytes correctly (`09df 081c 7047`), but the
  following chunk starts at 0x020043ae and everything from there shifts by two
  bytes: retail has `b9 1d` at 0x020043ae, the rebuild has `00 00`. The thumb
  object's section is padded to a 4-byte size, so a unit ending on a halfword
  boundary overwrites the next chunk. Nine of the ten stubs are at addresses
  that are 2 mod 4 (`0x020040e6`, `0x02004146`, `0x0200426a`, `0x020042d2`,
  `0x02004336`, `0x0200440e`, `0x02004466`, `0x020044ea`, `0x0200470a`); `Mod`
  is the only 4-aligned one and fails for the same padding reason.
* `align:2` cannot be expressed per chunk: dsd rejects it with "attribute
  'align' should be omitted as it is inherited from this file's header", and
  relaxing that header (`.text ... align:32` -> `align:2`) changes nothing
  because the attribute is not inherited by the gap objects.
* The unblocker was build-side, as suspected, and it needs two halves because
  mwld's generated script says `ALIGNALL(4)` inside `.arm9`:
  1. `tools/align_code.py` sets every executable progbits section of every
     linked object from `sh_addralign` 4 to 2, and
  2. the same step rewrites that one `ALIGNALL(4);` in the generated
     `arm9.lcf` to `ALIGNALL(2);` (the data sections keep their 4).
  `configure.py` runs it between the last object and the link, so the delinked
  sizes - which already equal retail's - become the whole layout. With both in
  place `Mod` links `09df 081c 7047` at 0x020043a8 followed by retail's `b91d`
  at 0x020043ae, and the arm9 code region has zero differing halfwords.
* Sources: `swi N` alone is a 4-byte stub (`svc` + `bx lr`); `mov r2, #0;
  swi 0x05` is VBlankIntrWait (`00 22 05 df 70 47`). Note the spelling: `mov`,
  not `movs`, or the encoding differs.

    addlt'd pointer; mwcc reorders or folds. 2 spellings.





0x02026a88 (28B) / 0x02026aa4 (36B) / 0x0202474c (36B): the ldm/stm copy family. Retail is





    N x (ldm r0!,{r2,r3,ip}; stm r1!,...) - 3-word chunks. Tried: word loop (60B, loop not





    unrolled), unrolled 12-byte struct assignment (68/84/92B, ldr/str each), memcpy (call, 28B).





    mwcc 1.2 never emits ldm/stm for any of these spellings; the idiom that produced it is unknown.





0x0202de8c (28B): ldrh/test/clear at 0x027fff96, every spelling differs in all 28 bytes.





0x02024b5c (48B): `*p |= 0x80000000 >> a0` after a two-way `p++/a0 -= 0x60|0x40`. Retail keeps





    `mov r1,#0x80000000 / lsr r1,r1,r0 / orr r2,r2,r1`; mwcc always folds the shift into the





    `orr` (44B). Tried a temp mask, split RMW through a value variable, and a volatile mask





    (64B). Also note retail uses PL/MI where `s32 a0 >= 0x60` gives GE/LT - the condition





    spelling differs, not just the fold.





0x0203b4e4 (40B): retail saves the argument block with `push {r0,r1,r2,ip,lr}` and restores it





    before a `bic/eor` on the callee's result; mwcc allocates r4/r5 and a frame (56B). Tried a





    no-arg and a 4-arg callee declaration. Argument-block push is a codegen choice, not source.





0x0203684c (40B): struct-by-value in r0/r1, retail copies it to the stack and clears bit 31 of





    `((u32 *)&s)[1]` (add r0,sp,#0 / add r1,r0,#4 / ldr / bic / str) then reloads both words for





    the return in r0/r1. Plain `s.b &= ~0x80000000; return s;` keeps everything in registers





    (36B); the explicit `((u32 *)&s)[1]` spelling spills wrong (44B). Retail is memory-resident,





    mine is register-resident, in both directions.

## 2026-11-21 leaf pass (tools/match/leafq.py): two mid-size parks





arm9 0x0202ad9c 108B -- sound-channel MMIO writer. Five address groups: 0x04000290/+4,





  0x04000298/+4, 0x04000280 (u16), 0x040002b8/+4, 0x040002b0 (u16); sources are a0[0..5]





  plus the u16s at a0+0x18/+0x1a. Retail is frameless with 5 pool words, each base serving





  1-2 stores with the second folded to +4. Tried: one base pointer p=0x04000290 with





  p[0..3],p[10],p[11] (92B); five literal addresses (132B, 8 pool words); five named





  pointer locals (124B + phantom 8-byte frame); one reused pointer d (124B + frame);





  per-address [0]/[1] indexing with no locals (132B). Every frameless spelling lands on





  7-8 pool words instead of 5; grouping the addresses costs a register and mwcc frames.





arm9 0x0201aaa0 108B -- DMA-ish setup around data_020475c4. Body (frameless, 23 insns):





  data_020475c4 = a0; if (a0[0][5]) return; [0x04000440]=0; [0x04000444]=0;





  [0x04000470]=-a0[0][0]; [0x04000470]=-a0[0][1]; [0x04000470]=0; [0x04000440]=1;





  Best 104B with p=a0[0] cached - retail re-derefs a0[0] for the second group, which is the





  missing 4th byte. Every spelling that re-derefs a0[0] (alone or mixed with p) makes mwcc





  emit `stmdb sp!,{lr}` + `sub sp,#4` and grow to 136B although the body has no call and





  never touches [sp]. Same phantom-frame class as arm9 02017954 / 0201779c.





arm9 0x0202ae08 116B -- getter twin of 0x0202ad9c: reads the same five sound registers into





  p[0..5] plus the two masked halves at p+0x18 (0x04000280 & 3) and p+0x1a (0x040002b0 & 1).

## 2026-11-21 residual relocation pass: two parks





  data_ov003_020e6fe4 passed as arg 0 and 0x800 as the length. Retail keeps NO





  callee-saved register: it re-loads the address from the pool before each call





  (ldr r0,[pc] x4 over one shared pool word) and its frame is the bare





  stmdb {lr}/sub sp,#4 pair. Every symbol spelling tried (u8[] with (u32)data,





  (u32)(u8*)data, (u32)&data, extern u8 *, extern u32) makes mwcc CSE the address





  into r4 and push it: 88B with r4 for the address forms plus a shifted pool, or





  108B when the address is a loaded variable. The pass sees the constant as one





  value; retail saw four. Needs a spelling that defeats that CSE without adding a





  variable.





  3 pool/reloc word identical, but registers permute (retail keeps the address in





  r0 and the pointer in lr, uses r2=n/ip=m/r3=i; every declaration order tried puts





  the pointer in r2 and n in r3, 108B in both). A pure register-assignment residue,





  not a spelling error; the object already carries the right relocation.





## 2026-11-21 Ghidra pass on arm9 leaves: three parks





arm9 0x02037788 124B -- bit-run scanner over four word parameters copied to the stack





  (`push {r0,r1,r2,r3}`, `u8 *p = (u8 *)&a`). Retail branches to a shared tail for the

  that converted block.





arm9 0x02037718 112B -- table walk over p[5+idx], length p[4], tail test `(p[idx+4] & 1)`.





  Body matches instruction for instruction except the tail, where retail emits





  `sub r1,r1,#1; add r0,r0,r1; ldrb r0,[r0,#5]` and every spelling of the same address





  (`p[idx+4]`, `p[idx-1+5]`, `(p+idx-1)[5]`, via a local) emits

arm9 0x020049a0 112B -- flush routine over the five-word table at data_02004a0c: per





  3-word entry copy `cur[1]` bytes from the shared source, then clear `cur[2]` bytes,





  then clear the tail range `[tab[3], tab[4])`. Retail's copy loop compares





  **signed** (`mi`) and its clear loops compare **unsigned** (`lo`), and it walks the





  table with post-increment loads (`ldr r4,[r1],#4`). The int-arithmetic spelling needed





  to get `mi` blocks the post-index walk, and the pointer spelling gives `lo` on the copy





  loop: 164B against 112B. Needs the mixed signed/unsigned shape expressed without





  losing the table walk.





## 2026-11-22 b56-neighbour probe (arm9 0x02018330, 124B)





First test of the "compiler version is a per-region property" idea: this unit sits 0x18 from





0x02018318, a b56 win. The body is a 3-argument reserve/shrink with a call to the stmlt/ldmlt





helper 0x020048e8. Both 1.2/b56 and 1.2/sp2 give 108B, so the compiler is not what is missing





here; the residue is that retail spills a zero through the stack before the call -





`mov r0,#0; str r0,[sp]; ldr r0,[sp]; mov r1,r4; bl` - while mwcc folds the constant argument





straight into r0. Tried: `volatile u32 z = 0`, a plain `u32 z = 0`, an address-taken `u32 z[1]`,





and the flag read spelled as `((u8 *)p)[-4]` and `((u16 *)p)[-2]`; all 108B under both compilers.





The flag field itself is a u8 at p-4 (retail does `ldr r0,[r5,#-4]; and r0,#0xff`).





## 2026-11-22 solved: ov008 0x0204d970 (108B, was 99.81%)





The last below-100% unit. The allocation was never wrong - the source was still writing the





raw address `*(u8 **)0x0204f440`, so the object held a literal where retail holds a relocation,





and every earlier attempt to introduce the symbol rewrote the expression to `g = sym`, which





changed the allocation to the swapped shape and looked like an unfixable tie. Keeping the





dereference and making the operand an address constant is the fix:











    extern u8 data_ov008_0204f440[];      /* array, not a pointer variable */





    u8 *g = *(u8 **)data_ov008_0204f440;











That compiles to retail's exact registers (g in lr, n in r2) and emits the ABS32 relocation





the delinked object expects. Lesson for the class: when swapping a literal for a symbol, keep





the expression tree identical (same casts, same dereference); only the operand should change.





arm9 0x02017880 104B -- list insert: p[0], (u16*)((u8*)p+10) offset, count at (u8*)p+8.





  Retail loads the offset halfword twice (+36, +64) and p[0] twice (+52, +68), and keeps





  p+8 in r2 from +40. Every spelling tried CSEs one more load than retail, giving 96 B





  (one shared local) or 104 B with three loads of the halfword and a different register





  plan. Needs a form that blocks CSE for exactly two of the three uses.

arm9 0x02028920 / 0x02028958 56B -- add a delta into a struct word, then call a function pointer.





  Retail: lr = e+0x28, ip = *lr, r4 = e->obj(+8), r2 = e->f28, r1 = e->f2c, *lr = ip + e->f34,

arm9 0x0201f3d0 104B -- bit-2 gate then re-init. Retail tests with lsl/asr on the flags word

ov003 0x02066a54 / 0x020b85b8 / 0x020db6ec 180B -- engine-B init: clear 0x200 u16 slots and





  write 0x200 as a word every 4th index, after two library calls and a DISPCNT(0x04001000) RMW

      u16 i;  ... for (i = 0; i < 0x200; i++) {

                   if (i % 4 == 0) *(u32 *)(g + 0x8de8 + i * 2) = 0x200; }





  with `if (mode == 0) { ... } else { ... }` and `extern u8 *data_020eb720;`. Retail puts the

  always allocates it to lr instead, dropping one push. Every other instruction matches, so this

ov005 0x020614a4 / 0x020656d8 / 0x020695bc / 0x0206bfc0 220B -- same engine-B init as the ov003





  0x02066a54 family with different offsets (clear 0x200 u16 slots at g+0x4a58, word 0x200 every

  func_02026a50(g + 0x4658, 0x7000000, 0x400); }. The mode!=0 path does nothing and falls into the





  shared `return 0` (there is no else body).





  Shape that matches everything except one scratch pair (220 B):

  With `p` as a local the counter does land in r5 as retail does, and the whole loop, the tail and

  1.2/sp3-sp4 216, 2.0/* 200, dsi/* 204), so this is not a compiler-version artifact.

arm9 0x02022554 84B -- NOT a boundary artefact: a four-argument sound-register writer whose





  arguments sit in r0/r1/r2/r3, which is why the body appeared to read undefined registers.





  0x02022554 is 72 bytes of code and the last 12 are its pool {0x04000060, 0xcf7f, 0x0400035c}.





  Shape that reproduces the whole function (84 B), registers and instruction order included:











      void func_02022554(u32 flag, u32 a1, u32 a2, u32 a3)





      {





          u32 v;





          if (flag != 0) {





              *(volatile u16 *)0x04000060 = (u16)a3;





              v = (a2 << 8) | (a1 << 6) | 0x80;





              *(volatile u16 *)0x0400035c = (*(volatile u16 *)0x0400035c & ~0x3f40) | v;





          } else {





              *(volatile u16 *)0x04000060 &= 0xcf7f;





          }





      }











  Two required details: `v` must be u32 (`u16` adds lsl/lsr 0x10), and the guard must be written





  inverted with the mask in the `else` so it if-converts into ldreq/strheq/bxeq.





  Residue: only the two `ldr [pc, ...]` imm12s. Positional pool layout is





  retail {0x04000060, 0xcf7f, 0x0400035c} vs mwcc {0x04000060, 0x0400035c, 0xcf7f}; explicit





  RHS, `&=`, and a mask temporary all keep mwcc's order. Nothing else differs.





ov002 0x0207ccd4 80B -- bounds test with optional mirroring; six arguments, the last two on the





  stack (r0 mirrored, r1 pair*, r2 x0, r3 y0, [sp] x1, [sp+4] y1). It negates the y bounds when





  `mirrored == 0`, writing one of them back into the incoming stack slot, then tests p->f0 and





  p->f4 against both ranges and returns 1/0.





  Closest shapes, both exactly 80 B:





      s32 ok = 1; ... if (p->f0 < x0 || p->f0 > x1 || p->f4 < y0 || p->f4 > y1) ok = 0; return ok;





      if (p->f0 >= x0 && p->f0 <= x1 && p->f4 >= y0 && p->f4 <= y1) return 1; return 0;





  Differences left: retail negates into `ip` and reuses `r3` for the second negation, and sets





  `mov r0,#1` before the checks with a final `bxle lr`; mwcc negates in place, keeps `movle r0,#1`





  plus `bxle lr` at the end, and reuses `r0` where retail uses `ip`. Also worth checking: the





  y bounds may be (y1, y0) in the source, since retail stores -y0 into the y1 slot.

arm9 0x020208b0 / 0x0202093c 140B each -- backlight/power mode setter on data_02048c74

      {

  data_02048c74 and the DISPCNT blocks. Declaration swap (lo first), `(u16)~a0`, `~(u32)a0` and





  the no-local form all keep mwcc's assignment; 1.2/b56 and 1.2/sp2 emit the same five words.





ov003 0x020d72f8 / 0x020d9f2c 164B each (famsurvey pair, 328B total) -- module reset routine.





  Body, exact at 164 B with the single-return structure below:





      s32 f(s32 a0)





      {





          u8 *b = data_ov003_020eb720;





          u16 i;





          if (a0 == 0) {





              func_020209c8(8);





              func_020208b0(0x100);





              *(volatile u32 *)0x04001000 = (*(volatile u32 *)0x04001000 & 0xffcfffef) | 0x100010;





              for (i = 0; i < 0x200; i++) {





                  u8 *p = b + i * 2;





                  *(u16 *)(p + 0x8de8) = 0;





                  if (i % 4 == 0)





                      *(u32 *)(p + 0x8de8) = 0x200;





              }





          }





          return 0;





      }





  Two levers were needed and both are now known: the early-out must be an `if (a0 == 0) { body }`





  with one shared `return 0` (writing `if (a0 != 0) return 0;` makes mwcc duplicate the epilogue





  at the early exit and costs 12 B -> 176), and the u16 loop counter makes `i % 4` promote to





  int, which is what produces retail's signed-division idiom (lsr #31 / rsb / ror #30 / adds)





  rather than `and #3`; the `i < 0x200` test stays `blo` because i is u16.





  Residue: the two loop scratch temps are swapped. Retail keeps the hoisted zero constant in lr





  (`mov lr, r5`, `strh lr, [r0, #0xe8]`) and the row pointer in ip (`add ip, r4, r5, lsl #1`,





  `add r0, ip, #0x8d00`, `addeq r0, ip, #0x8000`); mwcc puts the zero in ip and the pointer in





  lr. Six instructions, same length, no scheduling difference. Eight spellings tried: pointer





  local, pointer assigned in a separate statement, `p += i * 2`, plain `while`, u32-typed base





  with `0x8de8/4` index, `u32 z = 0` at function scope, `u16 z`, and `u32 j = i * 2` as a





  separate index - all 164 B with the same swap. Same ip/lr-preference class as 0x020208b0.





ov003 0x02082220 / 0x020c806c 96B each (famsurvey pair) -- DEGENERATE, no source expresses it.





  Retail: `cmp r1,#0x5a ; movlt r3,#0x1e ; movlt r2,#0x3c ; blt join ; cmp r1,#0xb4 ;





  movlt r3,#0x1e ; movlt r2,#0x3c ; movge r3,#0x1e ; movge r2,#0x3c ; join: cmp r3,#0 ;





  addeq/moveq/ldmeq/bxeq` then `mul r0,r2,r1 ; mov r1,r3 ; bl func_0203b0c0 ; lsl #0x10 ;





  asr #0x10`. All three arms of the if/else-if chain assign the same pair (30, 60), so the





  `cmp r3,#0` test after them is dead, yet retail keeps both the three-armed chain and the test.





  Written literally that way, mwcc folds the chain to one `movlt/movge` pair and drops the test





  (72 B). The lsl/asr #16 pair also implies the declared return is s32 with an explicit (s16)





  cast, not an s16 return. Same class as ov003 0x02050f2c (dead compare kept by retail): the





  retail body cannot come from the visible semantics, so it stays parked.





ov003 0x02066a54 / 0x020b85b8 / 0x020db6ec 180B each (famsurvey 3x180, 540B total) -- same module





  reset as 0x020d72f8 but with an else arm: `if (a0 == 0) { func_020209c8(8); func_020208b0(0x100);





  DISPCNT_SUB = (DISPCNT_SUB & 0xffcfffef) | 0x100010; clear loop as above } else





  func_ov003_0204e46c(*(u16 *)(b + 0x86c2)); return 0;`. Shape reproduces exactly at 180 B





  (including the `b` to the shared tail) with the same ip/lr swap in the loop: retail keeps the





  hoisted zero in lr and b+i*2 in ip, mwcc the reverse. Blocked by the same allocator preference





  as 0x020d72f8, so all five units in this cluster (2x164 + 3x180 = 868 B) stand or fall together.





ov001 0x0204d8f4 148B -- VCOUNT vs BG scroll setup. Full body: `v = *(volatile u16 *)0x04000006;





  q = (u32 *)(data_027c0000 + 0x3ff8); *q |= 2; g = data_ov001_02051cc0;





  if (!(g[0xa] & 4)) return; t = v < 0x3a ? 0x3a - v : v > 0x72 ? v - 0x72 : -1;





  if (t < 0) { *(volatile u16 *)0x04001052 = 0x10; return; }





  *(volatile u16 *)0x04001052 = t < 0x11 ? (u16)((0x10 - t) | (t << 8)) : 0x1000;`





  Body reproduces exactly except the DTCM flag: retail pools 0x027c0000 and 0x3ff8 SEPARATELY





  and materialises the sum (`ldr r1,=0x027c0000 ; ldr r0,=0x3ff8 ; add r2,r1,r0`), mwcc folds them





  into one pooled absolute 0x027c3ff8 (136-140B). Four spellings tried: `(u32 *)(sym + 0x3ff8)`,





  `u8 *b = sym; (u32 *)(b + 0x3ff8)`, `(u32 *)sym + 0xffe`, and a scalar `extern u32` with





  `&sym` - every one pools the single absolute. Same two-pool class as ov003 0x0205299c.





arm9 0x0202474c 36B -- four 12-byte block copies with a fixed destination (`ldm r0!,{r2,r3,ip}` /





  `stm r1,{r2,r3,ip}` x4). mwcc always keeps the source pointer in ip and the destination in r3,





  emits `mov ip,r0 / mov r3,r1` and rematerialises `add r0, ip, #0xc` between copies (56B).





  Tried: unrolled `*dst = src[0..3]` with volatile dst, the same with `*src++`, and a real





  4-iteration loop (mwcc adds a counted loop and a frame). Retail's ldm writeback means mwcc





  would have to keep the source in r0 across all four copies; no spelling does.





arm9 0x02026aa4 / 0x02026ac8 36B / 44B -- same 12-byte block copies with both pointers advancing





  (0x02026ac8 adds a final 16-byte copy). Same ip/r3 source-copy residue as 0x0202474c, plus a





  `stmdb sp!` frame from the pointer pair.





arm9 0x0203684c 40B -- `push {r0,r1,r2,r3}` then `q.w1 &= ~0x80000000` through the spilled stack





  slot, returning [sp] and [sp+4] in r0/r1. An 8-byte struct return makes mwcc use r0 as the sret





  pointer; a `u64` return gives the right registers but mwcc folds the mask without the stack





  write-back and adds two `orr #0` conversions (32B). Not reachable from either type.





ov008 0x0204cc94 140B -- slot allocator: reads `slot = *(u32 *)(p + 0xc10 + idx * 4)` where





  `p = *(u8 **)data_ov008_0204f440 + 0x24554` and `idx = *(u8 *)(base + 0x40)`, returns 0 when





  `slot >= 0x40`, else writes three u32s at `p + 0xc18 + idx * 0x300 + slot * 12` and





  post-increments the same count word. Retail materialises `base + 0x40` in one register and





  loads the index byte TWICE before the guard (r5 for the counts, r4 for the table), letting the





  base die; mwcc folds 0x40 into `ldrb` and CSEs the two index reads into one (136B). Tried:





  guard-first (144B: spills to r7 plus a stack slot), table-before-guard (132B), explicit





  `u8 *hdr = base + 0x40` pointer (136B: hoists the `add` past the guard and reloads through the





  folded address). Residue is allocation/CSE, not the shape.





ov008 0x0204d9dc 124B -- direction/percentage update: `if (flags == 0 || flags == 0x40) dir *= -1;`





  then `flags += dir; v = (flags * 10) / 64;` and two strh into `func_ov008_0204ed50()`'s object





  (+6 = 0x90, +0xa = v), with v computed BEFORE the call (retail keeps it in a callee-saved





  register). Fields are `u8` at base+0xd and `s8` at base+0xe. Retail materialises `base+0xe`





  inside the negate block and `base+0xd` inside the add block (`add r2/r3, r4, #k`) and uses





  immediate offsets for the plain loads; mwcc either folds all of them into the ldrb/strb





  (112B) or, with `u8 *flags = base+0xd; s8 *dir = base+0xe;` locals, hoists both `add`s above





  the compares (124B, allocation differs). Volatile on the RMWs does not change either behaviour.





  Same class as 0x0204cf80 below: retail's `add` for a byte RMW carries the branch predicate.





ov008 0x0204cf80 136B -- metre/slot picker: `if (*(u8 *)(base+0x30) < 0xc) ++` the byte, then





  `v = (*(u8 *)(base+0x30) * 0x10) / 12` (`smull 0x2aaaaaab` + `asr #1`, i.e. /12 on a*16),





  `if (a0 == 0) v = 0x10 - v;`, then `obj = func_ov008_0204ed50()`, `*(u16*)(obj+6) = 0x150`,





  `*(u16*)(obj+8) = (0x10 - v) | (v << 8)`, return `*(u8*)(base+0x30) == 0xc`.





  Nearest spelling: `u8 *cnt = &base[0x30]; if (*cnt < 0xc) (*cnt)++;` with the rest reading





  `*(u8 *)(base + 0x30)` - 136B, every instruction identical except the address: retail emits





  `cmp r1, #0xc` then `addlo r2, r5, #0x30`, mwcc emits the `add` unconditionally before the





  `cmp` and predicates only the ldrb/strb. Tried: plain expression (folds to immediate, 128B),





  volatile expression (folds, 132B), pointer declared inside the `if` (folds, 128B), inverted





  `if/else` (hoists, 132B). One instruction of predication decides the unit.





ov009 0x0204db84 140B -- two packed-field updates on a u16 pair: `p[0] = (p[0] & 0xff00) |





  ((a2 - v.hi) & 0xff)` and `p[1] = (p[1] & 0xfe00) | ((a1 - v.lo) & 0x1ff)` with `if (p == 0)





  return;` before any use; 0x1ff is pooled (word at 0x0204dc0c). `v` is a 4-byte struct passed





  by value (u16 at +0, s16 at +2) - that is why retail starts with `push {r0,r1,r2,r3}` (all





  four argument registers homed). Retail then adds `push {r4,r5,lr}` + `sub sp,#4` and reads both





  halves back from the home space (`ldrsh r4,[sp,#0x1e]` hoisted above the null check,





  `ldrh r5,[sp,#0x1c]`), while mwcc homes the registers but keeps the struct in r3 and shifts it





  (`lsl/lsr`), giving 100B. By-value spellings cannot ask for the home-space reload; the shape





  is recorded here so a later pass can try an explicit `&v` or a caller-side layout.





ov004 0x02050068 100B -- same instance-table walk as the matched 0x02050154, but it calls the





  function pointer at table+0x8 when the ids match, writes -1 to table+0x18 and returns 1.





  `for (i = 0; i < *(s32 *)p; i++)` with `*(u32 *)(p + 0xc) == *(u32 *)(((u32 *)(p + 0x20))[i]





  + 0x98)` reproduces the instruction sequence exactly at 100B (the scaled `add r0, r4, ip,





  lsl #2` plus `ldr [r0, #0x20]` needs the inline `((u32 *)(p + 0x20))[i]` form - a `u8 *`





  byte-offset expression keeps a second counter, a `u32 *tab` local gets hoisted into ip). The





  only residue is the allocation: retail uses count=r2, cached +0xc=r3, counter=ip; mwcc gives





  count=r1/r3, cached=r2, counter=r3/r1. Four spellings (inline count, cached count local,





  both hoisted as locals, declared in retail's order) all choose the other assignment, so this





  is the register-tie wall, not a shape problem.





ov004 0x0204d8ec 128B -- binary search over the same overlay's table: `if (key >





  *(u16 *)(data_ov004_020630c0 + 0x54)) return 0x6b;` then `while (hi >= 0 && lo <= hi)` with





  `hi = 0x2d5` initially, `mid = (lo + hi) / 2` (signed `/2` gives retail's `add r1, r4, lr /

  data_ov004_020625c0, `hi = mid - 1` / `lo = mid + 1` predicated, 0x6b on a miss.





  Nearest spelling is 136B: mwcc duplicates the `mov r0, #0x6b; pop; bx lr` epilogue (one copy





  after the range guard, one after the loop) where retail branches both exits to a single copy





  (`bhi`, then `blt`/`ble` to the shared one at the end) and enters the loop through a `b test`.





  Tried: tab local before the guard (136B), tab inline after the guard (136B), single-exit





  `r = 0x6b; ... break; return r` (136B). The rotation/duplication is mwcc's, not the source's.





ov004 0x0204fb74 124B -- AABB overlap test: `h = ((*(u16 *)(a0+2) & 0x3f) << 2) << 12`,





  `dx = a1[5] - a2[1]`, `dy = a1[4] - a2[0]`, then `dx + h > 0`, `dx - h < a2[3]`,





  `dy + h > 0`, and the result is `dy - h < a2[2]`. The count field is masked twice at byte





  width (`and #0x3f`, `and #0xff`, `lsl #2`, `and #0xff`), so the source has two u8 steps:





  `u8 a = *(u16 *)(a0 + 2) & 0x3f; u8 n = a << 2;`. Residue: retail repeats the shift at all

  register operands, 132B. Tried: early `return 0` chain (if-converted to movle/ldmle/bxle,





  164B), single `r = 0` result with a chained `&&` condition (132B), same with an explicit





  `s32 h` local (132B), `u32 *` vs `s32 *` for the two record pointers (same). The 8 bytes are





  the CSE'd shift plus its scheduling.

arm9 0x0203b50c 76B -- the packed-field sibling of 0x0203b4e4: repack both arguments as





  `(v & 0x1f) | ((v & 0x1f0000) >> 8)` first - which retail does exactly, in place on r0/r1 - then





  the same lock-word `(old & ~a0) ^ a1` and return





  `(old & 0x1f) | ((old & 0x1f00) << 8) | 0x40000000`. Same spill wall as 0x0203b4e4 (92B against





  76B); the packing half is already byte-identical.





arm9 0x0203b698 56B -- call every non-null entry of a function-pointer table:





  `while (t != 0) { if (*t == 0) break; (*t)(); t++; }` with the table address from a literal pool





  (`ldr r4, [pc]; cmp r4, #0` - the address itself is compared against zero, which no plain





  `T **t = table;` spelling produces), `blx r0` for the call and the table pointer bumped by 4.





  Not attempted past the decode.





ov002 0x02066080 112B -- per-entity limit check: copy the 12-byte table at data_ov002_020fdc24 to a





  stack local (`ldm`/`stm`), then `b = *(u8 **)data_ov002_02102070 + 0x1744 + a0 * 0x38` and





  `if (*(u32 *)(b + 0x18) < t.v[*b]) { *(u32 *)(b + 0x18) += 1; return 0; } return 1;`. Two





  findings: the reload needs `<volatile u32>` on the field (otherwise mwcc CSEs the compare load





  into the increment and the unit is 4B short), and the entity pointer must be computed *before*





  the table copy or the r0-r3 allocation is scrambled. 112/112 with only the RMW addressing form





  left: retail uses `ldrlo r1, [r2, #0x18]!` + `strlo r1, [r2]` (pre-indexed writeback), mwcc





  always emits `[r2, #0x18]` twice. Local pointer, `++`, and the split-expression form all give





  the same pair.

  statements (`e = base + 0x274; e = e + idx * 0x1c;`) and the field has its own `u32 *v` - that





  reproduces retail's `mla lr, r4, r3, lr` exactly. The remaining difference is the eq branch:

  declaration with sh inside, and the reverse; none reproduce the interleave.

  then while `a0[0x3d] >= p[0x3d]` re-seek with `func_02017784(data_020475fc, p)`, breaking on null,





  and finally `func_0201780c(data_020475fc, a0)`. Mine is 112B: mwcc caches the pooled address of





  data_020475fc in r4 and reuses it for all three calls, while retail reloads the literal from the





  pool at each of the three sites and only materialises r4 after the first null check.





arm9 0x020269ec 24B (and twin 0x0201cb84 family): retail is a word-copy loop written as





  `end = (u8 *)a1 + a2` (unscaled byte count), test at the TOP of the body with the pair PREDICATED





  (`cmp r1, ip` / `ldmlt r0!, {r2}` / `stmlt r1!, {r2}` / `blt`), and the end pointer in `ip`.





  Both 1.2 compilers refuse the if-conversion: every `while`/`for` spelling gives a pre-check branch





  (`bxhs lr`, 32B) and every `do{}while` spelling gives 24B with the test at the BOTTOM and the end





  pointer in r3. Tried: u32*/u8* pointers, byte-count vs u32 count, `*a1++ = *a0++` and separate





  increments, a 1-word struct `Cell` copy, and for/while/do-while with each. Also no spelling





  produced retail's single-register `ldm`/`stm` copy (always `ldr`/`str` with writeback).





arm9 0x02037660 100B -- limits a byte field: `if (a1 <= 0) return; if (a1 >= a0[4]) return;





  if (func_02037718(a0) < 0) { a0[4] = a1; return; } func_020376c4(a0, a1);`. Size-exact at 100/100





  with the field read through a local, through `*(u8 *)(a0 + 4)`, and with the two guards merged into

arm9 0x0203b4e4 40B -- `p = func_0203b6d0(a0, a1, a2); old = *p; *p = (old & ~a0) ^ a1; return old;`.

  pointer parked in r3 across the pop, i.e. a frame-less save of the incoming argument registers.

arm9 0x02028920 56B + twin 0x02028958 -- `obj = *(u8 **)(a0 + 8); cur = *(u32 *)(a0 + 0x28);

arm9 0x020248c8 72B -- IME/IE critical-section bit clear, 56/72 with a *byte-identical body*. Correct C:





  `saved = *(volatile u16 *)0x04000208; *(volatile u16 *)0x04000208 = 0;





  *(volatile u32 *)0x04000210 = *(volatile u32 *)0x04000210 & ~mask;





  r = *(volatile u16 *)0x04000208; *(volatile u16 *)0x04000208 = saved; return r;`





  (the read of IME after zeroing it, returned as-is, is real: retail does ldrh/strh there and the





  value is always 0). That gives retail's 14 instructions exactly, at 56B. Retail's extra 16 bytes are





  a prologue/epilogue pair with NO stack references at all: `stmdb sp!, {lr}` + `sub sp, sp, #4` and





  `add sp, sp, #4` + `ldm sp!, {lr}`. An unused 4-byte frame. Any Ctx/struct local that forces a frame





  also changes the schedule (reading IE early is what keeps two values live and the frame alive, and





  that moves the `ldr` of IE before `strh` of IME, which retail does not do). b56 and sp2 both give 56.





  Note: a 4-byte frame with no stack references is usually the second half of an 8-byte AAPCS





  alignment pair (see `arm9 0x0201f21c`, matched under 1.2/b56); this unit is a leaf and b56 does not





  align it, so that explanation does not apply here.

arm9 0x02026ac8 44B -- 64-byte block copy, 60/44. Retail copies in 3-word chunks with the parameters as

  r0,r2,r3,ip). mwcc 1.2 always picks 4-word chunks for a struct assignment (and spills a frame to

arm9 0x02024b04 88B -- bitmap allocator; NOT writable in C. Body is `clz` on two words of





  0x02027ffb0, allocating the set bit in the first word (id 0x40+n) or second (0x60+n), returning





  0xfffffffd when both are exhausted, and clearing the bit with 0x80000000 >> n. `__cntlzw`, `__clz`,





  `_clz`, `__builtin_clz`, `__CLZ` and `__clz32` all compile to an unresolved external call under both





  1.2/b56 and 1.2/sp2, so the `clz` instruction cannot come from C in this toolchain.

arm9 0x020124e0 68B -- **68/68 with the same instruction multiset**, only the schedule differs: retail
