arm9 0x02017954 64B -- register-role tie, not a shape problem: seven candidates reproduce the





  frame, both zero stores, both pointer stores and the u16 increment; retail keeps n in ip and





  allocates &count/zero first, mwcc keeps n in r3. See notes/mwccarm-codegen-log.md 2.80.











ov003 0x0205299c 76B -- 68 of 76 bytes agree; the residue is the last RMW's addressing.





  Retail: `ldr r1,[pc]=0x027c0000; ldr r0,[pc]=0x3ff8; ldr r0,[r1,r0]!; orr r0,r0,#1; str r0,[r1]`.





  Every candidate instead folds the two words into one pooled 0x027c3ff8 and emits





  `ldr r0,[pc]; orr; str r0,[r1]`, so the unit is 4 bytes short and the pool shifts.





  The first half matches exactly: `[0x020eb720]` -> `[that]` -> function pointer, called if





  non-zero. Nine shapes tried: `&data_027c0000 + 0x3ff8`, raw `0x027c3ff8`, both operands as





  literals, `u8 *base` / `u32 off` variables assigned before and after the call, `u32 *p`





  computed before the call, `volatile`, and `*p = *p | 1`. So this is the





  address-materialisation class (constants 0x027c0000 and 0x3ff8 kept in two registers with a





  pre-indexed writeback), not the logic.











=== ov002 entity helpers: semantics solved, register roles not (2026-11-22) ===





The shared global data_ov002_02102070 is a bss pointer to the entity array, stride 0x158





(the same stride the 756-byte family uses). Three small units reconstruct to the right size





with only register roles differing; permute.py over statement order scored best 4 bytes off.











ov002 0x02068c78 64B -- size exact, differs only in which of ip/r1/r2/r3 holds base/entity/q:





  u8 *e = data_ov002_02102070 + i * 0x158;   /* mla r2, r0, #0x158, base */





  u8 *q = data_ov002_02102070 + 0x1e6c + (((u32)e[0x2e6]) >> 1);





  q[0x680] = 1;  q[0x682] = i;  q[0x684] = 5;    /* r0 = 5 falls out of the last store */





  Retail keeps the base in ip and q in r1; b56 puts base in r3 and q in r2. The shift must be





  written `((u32)e[0x2e6]) >> 1` or mwcc emits asr instead of retail's lsr.











ov002 0x02081078 60B -- size exact, one register role and a 4-byte order residue:





  u8 *g = *(u8 **)&data_ov002_02102070;  u8 *p = g + i;





  if (p[0x3d] != 0) return;  func_ov002_02080edc();





  Retail loads g into r2 and keeps i in r0; b56 loads it into r1. Writing the element as





  g[i + 0x3d] instead folds 0x3d into the index and is further away.











ov002 0x02080ca4 68B -- table copy, differs in loop-register roles:





  u8 *p = func_ov002_020848a0();  s16 *tab = (s16 *)0x020fc68c;





  for (i = 0; i < 4; i++) { s16 v = tab[i]; *(u16 *)(p + 0x300 + i * 8 + 0xe6) = v; }





  The s16 load is required (retail ldrsh); a u16 load is ldrh and will not match. 0x020fc68c





  is not a symbol in symbols.txt, so it has to be used as a raw address.











=== arm9 Thumb BIOS stubs at 2-mod-4 addresses (alignment wall, 9 units / 38 B) ===











Every function in the ROM whose address is not 4-aligned is a Thumb BIOS wrapper, and all nine





are unmatched because they cannot be linked as separate units: mwccarm emits `.text` with





`sh_addralign = 4` no matter what (`__attribute__((aligned(2)))`, `#pragma align 2` and





`__declspec(align(2))` all still produce a 4-aligned section), the linker therefore pads two





bytes in front of the unit, and the arm9 image shifts. dsd rejects `align:2` on a delinks





sub-entry ("inherited from this file's header"). Retail must have kept these wrappers inside a





larger object with a 2-aligned boundary, so they can only be matched by co-locating with a





neighbour that is itself decompiled.











  Halt                           0x020040e6  4B   swi 0x06





  Sqrt                           0x02004146  4B   swi 0x0d





  RLUnCompReadNormalWrite8bit    0x0200426a  4B





  VBlankIntrWait                 0x020042d2  6B





  IsDebugger                     0x02004336  4B   swi 0x0f





  LZ77UnCompReadByCallbackWrite16bit 0x0200440e  4B





  CpuSet                         0x02004466  4B   swi 0x0b





  GetCRC16                       0x020044ea  4B   swi 0x0e





  (one more at 0x020045xx, run the symscan in notes/mwccarm-codegen-log.md 2.81 to list all)











All nine are byte-identical to retail under var3/compile_text; only placement blocks them.











=== ov003 0x02082220 / 0x020c806c, 96B x2 (identical twins) ===











Both are the same code byte for byte:











  s16 f(s32 unused, s32 x)





  {





      s16 a, b;





      if (x < 90)            { a = 30; b = 60; }





      else if (x < 180)      { a = 30; b = 60; }





      else                   { a = 30; b = 60; }





      if (a == 0) return 0;





      return (s16)func_0203b0c0(b * x, a);   /* arm9, size 0x20c */





  }











Retail keeps the second comparison (`cmp r1, #0xb4` with both `movlt` and `movge` arms) even





though all three arms assign the same constants. b56 folds the chain away and emits 80 bytes





unless the arms differ textually, and the only difference that survives to codegen is assignment





order: `b = 60; a = 30;` in one arm keeps the branch (96 bytes, size exact) but emits r2 before





r3, while retail emits r3 (a) before r2 (b) in every arm. Constant-spelling differences





(`0x1e` vs `30`, `15 + 15`) fold, so the source difference that made retail keep the branch is





something mwcc normalises *after* the front end — the branch is kept but the arms still emit





identical instruction streams. Not solvable by statement reshuffling; the two twins are 192 B.





The call target is arm9 `func_0203b0c0` (0x20c, matched status per symbols.txt).











=== ov003 0x020b6b60 / 0x020b732c, 52B x2 (dead-store zero-init) ===











Twelve bytes of stack zeroed as three s16 pairs, then `bx lr`; no call, no use of the values.





Any straight C form (`Pair a, b, c;` with members, `s16 a[2]` arrays, aggregate initialisers)





has its stores deleted at -O4 and compiles to `bx lr`. `volatile` keeps the stores but emits





`[sp]`-relative addressing instead of retail's `add r1, sp, #0` / `add r2, sp, #4` register





reuse. Retail's shape needs the aggregates to escape to something that does not appear in the





52 bytes, so the construct that produced it is still unknown. The two twins are 104 B.











=== small-unit sweep: nothing under 24 bytes is C (checked 2026-11-22) ===











Every unmatched unit of 20 bytes or less in arm9/ov003/ov006 was disassembled. All of them are





hand-written assembly or fragments, each blocked for a structural reason, so this size band is





exhausted:











  0x020040e6..0x020045xx  4-6B Thumb SWI stubs at 2-mod-4   (alignment section above)





  0x02033cb0  8B   udf #0xffff trap; not expressible in C





  0x0203b2cc  8B   cmp/bxeq then falls into the next symbol (non-returning fragment)





  0x0203aeb8  12B  same fragment class





  0x02024af8  12B  ldr r1,[pc]; bx r1 veneer





  0x020263c0  12B  subs/bhs flag-chained delay loop; C emits cmp+subs or sub without flags





                   (tried while, do-while, and returning n — 16/24 bytes, never 12)





  0x02048e8   20B  stm fill loop: add ip,r1,r2 / cmp / stmlt r1!,{r0} / blt





  0x0202612c  20B  two-call wrapper; mwcc pads 4 bytes for AAPCS stack alignment, retail does not





  0x0203b564  20B  svc #0x123456 wrapper (r0=3)





  0x0203b578  20B  svc #0x123456 wrapper (r0=7, r1=0)





  0x0203b58c  16B  svc #0x123456 wrapper (r0=0x18)





  0x02033bc0/0x02033c0c 16B  mov r0,#0 / mov r0,#1 thunks that branch into the next symbol





  0x0204df9c  12B  epilogue only: add sp,sp,#4 / ldm sp! / bx lr





  0x02050f2c  16B  cmp/moveq/movne both returning 0; folds to `bx lr` in every C form tried











Conclusion: no unit below 24 bytes can be installed, so the next real target is ordinary C in the





ov002 family clusters (13x756, 14x608, 10x688, 10x676), starting with one fully decoded member.











=== arm9 0x0201fce4 (28B) and 0x0201fd00 (24B): 2.0-built, register roles only ===











Twins of the matched func_0201fcc8 (log 2.82) in the same 2.0-built Thumb block. Both are





nine-word fixed-point block initialisers:











  fce4: p[0]=b, p[1]=0, p[2]=-a, p[3]=0, p[4]=0x1000, p[5]=0, p[6]=a, p[7]=0, p[8]=b





  fd00: p[0]=b, p[1]=a, p[2]=0, p[3]=-a, p[4]=b, p[5..7]=0, p[8]=0x1000











Evidence gathered:





* every one of the 25 vendored mwccarm builds was tried on the best source shape. 1.2/* give





  32/40 B (the 0x1000 becomes a pool load); 2.0/* give the right 28 B for fce4 but put the zero





  and the negation in r2 where retail uses r3 for the zero and reuses r2 for -a; dsi/* reorder





  the whole body.





* fce4 retail stores index 2 twice (0 then -a), which is what pointers guarantee (no dead-store





  elimination through a parameter). Nine statement orders were tried: the two that keep the





  double store land the zero in r2 as well, so the allocation never matches.





* fd00 retail writes through an incrementing pointer with `stm r0!` group stores





  ({r2}, {r1,r3}, {r1,r2}) and then immediate offsets for the tail; both 1.2 and 2.0 emit plain





  `str` for every form of the source tried (array, pointer increments, struct members).











So this is a source-shape problem, not a compiler one: retail keeps b live in r2 to the last store





and uses r3 as the zero/0x1000 register at the same time. 52 B total, parked until a shape that





forces that pairing turns up.











=== arm9 family survey pass (2026-11-22): four groups examined, none loadable ===











  arm9 0x02028920 / 0x02028958 (56B x2) -- virtual-call trampolines, 2.0-era style allocation





    f(o): u32 v = o[10]; o[10] = v + o[13]; (*(void(**)(u8*,u32,u32))(c+0x40))(c, o[11], v)





    where c = (u8 *)o[2]. Source reproduces instruction for instruction but mwcc prologues with





    `stmdb sp!, {lr} / sub sp, #4` where retail has `push {r4, lr}` and lr/ip pressed into service





    as temporaries (`add lr, r0, #0x28`, `ldr ip, [lr]`, `ldr ip, [r4, #0x40]` + `mov r0, r4`).





    Retail's allocation is what mwcc gives a C++ this-call with the callee pointer held in a





    callee-saved register; no C shape tried reproduces it (plain function pointer through the





    struct, vtable pointer, temp locals, reversed statement order).











  arm9 0x02023df8 / 0x02023e58 (96B x2) -- dispatcher differing only in one constant





    u32 f(u32 a, u32 b, u32 c) { u32 g = data_02042370;





        if (g != 0xffffffff) return func_020268e0(g, a, 0x6600000 + b);





        return func_020269ec(a, 0x6600000 + b); }





    (0x6400000 in the twin.) Body matches, but retail's prologue copies every argument first --





    `mov r5, r0 / mov r4, r1 / mov r3, r2` -- and computes the constant into ip before the test;





    mwcc emits 88B using lr for the link and no shuffles. The unused `mov r3, r2` in particular





    has no C origin: retail saves a third parameter that is never read afterwards.











  arm9 0x02033bd0 / 0x02033c1c (60B x2) -- mrs/msr CPSR mask toggles





    `mrs r3, apsr / mov r1, r3 / and r1, r1, #0x40 / ... / msr cpsr_fc, r3 / tst r1,r1 /





    moveq r0,#1 / movne r0,#0` (0x80 in the twin). Hand-written assembly; no C form.











  arm9 0x02023df8 group also confirms the pattern from the sub-24B sweep: what is left in the





  small arm9 bands is SDK assembly, compiler-shim glue and C++-style calls, not lost C.











=== ov002 near-misses reduced to one instruction each (2026-11-22) ===











  ov002 0x0206d600 76B -- `*(u32 *)(base + 0x1dd20c) = v; *(u32 *)(...+4) = 0;





  func_020048e8(0, ...+8, 0x800);` with the zero forced into memory (`u32 zero = 0;` and





  `*(u32 *)&zero`) reproduces retail's frame, its `str r2,[sp]`/`ldr r0,[sp]` spill and its `bl`.





  Residue: mwcc writes the address with a pre-index store, `str r0, [rBase, rOff]!`, where retail





  has `add r1, r3, r1` then `str r0, [r1]`. Nine shapes tried (integer address, u32/struct index,





  separate pointer variables, store reordering, 84B struct-pad form); every one either folds into





  the pre-index store (76B, 1 instruction different) or over-computes to 84B.











  ov002 0x020882d4 76B -- `for (i = 0; i < 2; i++) if (ent[i].link != -1) { func_02006504(i);





  ent[i].link = -1; }` where ent is `*(u32 *)&data_ov002_02102070` and the field is at +0x1c.





  The exact retail shape (`add r0, base, i, lsl #2` recomputed on both sides of the call, then





  `[r0, #0x1c]`) is produced only by a 32-byte element struct, which gives `lsl #5` instead. Every





  4-byte-element spelling strength-reduces to an incrementing pointer (72B) and `b[i + 7]`





  hoists `i + 7` (80B). All 25 compilers were tried on the 72B shape: none avoids the reduction.


















  ov002 0x02082034 92B -- `u8 *p = func_ov002_020848a0(); for (i = 0; i < 4; i++)





  *(u16 *)(p + 0x300 + i * 8 + 0xc6) = data_ov002_020fc6e4[b[0x24] * 4 + i];` with





  b = `*(u8 **)&data_ov002_02102070 + 0x36ac`. The inner table access is solved: a 4-s16 row





  struct (`t[b[0x24]].f[i]`) reproduces retail's `add r2, ip, r2, lsl #3` + `ldrsh [r3, r2]`, but





  it costs an extra callee-saved register (108B). Best shape is 96B vs 92B, differing only in





  that retail materialises `b` with a pooled `add r4, r2, r1` where mwcc merges the constant into





  `add #0x3000` + `ldrb [r1, #0x6d0]`, plus one redundant zero init. A `u32 off = 0x36ac` local





  does not stop the merge.










  ov005 0x020614a4 220B (family of 4: also 0x020656d8, 0x020695bc, 0x0206bfc0) -- source fully




  recovered and instruction-identical except ONE register pair:




      u8 *base = *(u8 **)&data_ov005_020c9740;




      if (flag == 0) {




          func_020209c8(8); func_020208b0(0x100);




          *(u32 *)0x04001000 = (*(u32 *)0x04001000 & 0xffcfffef) | 0x00100010;




          for (i = 0; i < 0x200; i++) {           /* u16 i */




              u8 *p = base + i * 2;




              *(u16 *)(p + 0x4a58) = 0;




              if (i % 4 == 0) *(u32 *)(p + 0x4a58) = 0x200;




          }




          if (*(u16 *)(base + 0x4388) < 0x1f4) {




              func_02025c94(base + 0x4658, 0x400);




              func_02026a50(base + 0x4658, 0x7000000, 0x400);




          }




      }




      return 0;




  Residue: retail holds the loop pointer in `ip` and the stored zero in `lr`; mwcc b56 emits the




  opposite (`mov ip, r5` for the zero, `add lr, r4, r5, lsl #1` for the pointer), so 2 of 220




  bytes differ and reg.py reports miss. Swapping the two loop statements flips the pointer into




  `ip` as retail has it but then drops the r5 counter and the frame (212B). Struct/pointer




  spellings (u16*, u8*, index form), a `u16 zero`/`u16 z = 0` local, declaration order, and all




  25 vendored compilers (only 1.2/{b56,base,sp2,sp2p3} keep the 220B size) all leave the tie.




  Note retail's unused `sub sp, sp, #4` frame, reproduced only by the mwcc ordering above.








  ov003 0x02066a54 180B (family of 3: also 0x020b85b8, 0x020db6ec) -- SAME skeleton as the ov005



  family above (setup calls, HW register RMW, u16 zero-fill loop with i%4 kick, single return),



  with the `else` path calling func_02004e46c(*(u16 *)(base + 0x86c2)) instead of the two buffer



  calls. Source recovered, 180B, instruction-for-instruction identical EXCEPT the same ip/lr pair:



  retail `mov lr, r5` (stored zero) + `add ip, r4, r5, lsl #1` (loop pointer); mwcc b56 emits



  `mov ip, r5` + `add lr, r4, r5, lsl #1`. That the tie reproduces identically in two different



  overlays and functions means the shared source shape is still missing one detail that changes



  the allocator's colouring, not that either unit is mis-transcribed. Variants tried here:



  hoisting `i % 4` into a u32 local (same), u16* pointer with 0x8de8/2 index (184B).






=== arm9 small-unit sweep (2026-11-22) ===





  The four ldm/stm copy routines are library assembly, not C:


  0x02026a88 (28B, 3x `ldm r0!,{r2,r3,ip}` / `stm r1!,{r2,r3,ip}`), 0x0202474c (36B, 4x with


  `stm r1` no writeback), 0x02026aa4 (36B, 4x writeback), 0x02026ac8 (44B, 4x writeback plus a


  final `ldm r0` / `stm r1!` of 4 registers). No frame, no pool, data in r2/r3/ip with the


  pointers left in r0/r1. Struct-copy C (`*d++ = *s++` over a 3-u32 struct, three or four times)


  compiles to 68/84B with a `stmdb sp!` frame and r0/r1 as the data registers, in every spelling


  tried (const source, void* params, u32 casts). These stay assembly.





  0x02005418 32B -- `((u32 *)0x027c0000)[0xffe] |= 1;` (pool words 0x027c0000 and 0x3ff8).


  Retail: `ldr r1,[pc]` (base) ; `ldr r0,[pc]` (offset) ; `ldr r0,[r1,r0]!` ; `orr r0,r0,#1` ;


  `str r0,[r1]`. Every spelling that keeps both pool words gives 28B with `add r1, r0,#0x27c0000`


  instead of the pre-index writeback; spelling the address as one constant collapses to a single


  pool word and 24B.





  0x02024b5c 48B -- `n >= 0x60 ? (p = (u32*)0x027fffb0 + 1, n -= 0x60) : (n -= 0x40);


  p[0] |= 0x80000000u >> n;`. Retail materialises the mask with a separate `mov r1,#0x80000000 ;


  lsr r1,r1,r0` and then `ldr r2,[r3] ; orr r2,r2,r1 ; str r2,[r3]`. mwcc folds the register shift


  into the consumer (`orr r0, r2, r1, lsr r0`) for 44B in every spelling tried: `m = 0x80000000u


  >> n` then `|=`, `m >>= n`, `m = m >> n`, reversed operands `m | p[0]`, `1u << (31 - n)`, and


  every one of the 25 vendored compilers. Also note retail's condition codes here are `addpl` /


  `subpl` / `submi` where mwcc emits `addge` / `subge` / `sublt` for an `s32` parameter -- PL


  means the source's compare was proven non-overflowing (a narrowed/unsigned parameter), so the


  parameter type is still wrong; that type difference and the shift fusion are likely one cause.




  ov004 0x0204cbdc 32B is byte-identical to arm9 0x02005418 32B (same `|= 1` on

  (u32*)0x027c0000 + 0x3ff8), so whichever source cracks one cracks both. The struct-pad

  spelling (`struct { u8 pad[0x3ff8]; u32 f; }` at 0x027c0000) also gives 28B: mwcc pools the

  offset and adds the base as an immediate, where retail pools the base AND the offset and folds

  the add into a pre-index load (`ldr r0,[r1,r0]!`).



  arm9 0x0203684c 40B -- `T f(u32 a, u32 b)` returning a two-u32 struct with `b & ~0x80000000`.

  Retail spills r0-r3 (`push {r0,r1,r2,r3}`), masks [sp+4], then returns the pair in r0/r1

  (`ldr r0,[sp] ; ldr r1,[sp,#4] ; add sp,#0x10 ; bx lr`). Both `T f(T)` and `T f(u32,u32)`

  compile to a hidden-sret pointer (36B/32B) instead of a register pair, so the return ABI in the

  source still differs.



  arm9 0x0203b4e4 40B -- `p = func_0203b6d0(); v = *p; *p = (v & ~a) ^ b; return v;`. Retail

  brackets the call with `push {r0,r1,r2,ip,lr}` / `pop {r0,r1,r2,ip,lr}` and does the mask with

  `bic ip, r2, r0`, so no frame and no callee-saved registers. mwcc b56 always allocates

  r4/r5 + `sub sp,#4` (56B), with or without a prototype for the callee.


=== ov002 large families: Ghidra decode available ===

  0x0208f860 (13 members, 756B each; also 0x0209a5f4 0x0209c728 0x0209eb18 0x020a8ba0 0x020ad2b0
  ...) -- decompilation dumped to build/ghidra/out9.txt. Structure: entity record at
  base + 0x158*arg + 0x2e4 (base = *(u32*)data_ov002_02102070), fields +0x2e6 (byte, >>1),
  +0x128, +0x4c (bit 2 selects a branch), +0x50 (bits 0x10/0x20 shift a value up/down), +0x2e4
  and +0x300 read as call arguments. Calls in order: 0205bf64, 02034148(0x??48 literal),
  02053468, 0205bee4(0x1e, rec), 0204f770, 020532e4, 0204f6ac, 0203b0c0 (twice), 02057068
  (11 args, incl. constant 0x42000000 and 9), 020780f8, then clears bit 0 of *(u32*)(base+0x38).
  Contains a second pool-record pointer (base + iRam0208fb44) with fields [1] and [2] used as a
  height/limit and a 0x2d0000 term, and a counter loop bounded at 100 iterations. This is a
  multi-session transcription; the decompiler's division and loop reconstructions are wrong and
  the fixed-point `smull` block must be re-derived from the disassembly.



  arm9 0x020255b8 48B -- `for (i = 0; i < 0x10; i++) if (t[i + 5] == 0) return i; return -1;`
  with t = (u32 *)&data_02048d3c. Best shape is 48B and differs in exactly two instructions:
  mwcc emits `add r1, r0, #5` + `ldr r1, [r2, r1, lsl #2]`, retail emits `add r1, r2, r0, lsl #2`
  + `ldr r1, [r1, #0x14]`. Spelling the base-plus-offset first (`u32 *t = &data + 0x14; t[i]`)
  hoists the add out of the loop; the inline byte-offset form (`b + i*4 + 0x14`), a `u32 *row`
  local and `&t[i] + 0x14` all strength-reduce to an offset accumulator (56B). Same
  offset-folding family as ov002 0x020882d4.

  arm9 0x0203b4b0 52B -- `p = func_0203b6d0(); *p = 0; q = func_0203b6dc(); q[1..5] = 0;`.
  Retail has no frame at all (`stmdb sp!,{lr}` ... `ldm sp!,{lr}` around both calls). mwcc b56
  always inserts `sub sp, sp, #4` to 8-byte-align the SP at the calls, giving 60B in every
  spelling (one variable, two variables, single statement per store). Likely hand-written
  assembly paired with those two accessors.

  arm9 0x0202963c 80B -- linked-list find by key: `key = func_02029704(); func_02026350();
  for (n = head; n && n->id != key; n = n->next); func_02026364(); return n;` with
  head = *(Node **)0x0204abe8 and Node = {s32 id; Node *next;}. Seven spellings (for, while
  with break, while with &&, inverted test, do-loop) x six mwcc builds (1.2 b56/base/sp2,
  2.0 base/sp2/sp1p7) all give 84B: retail rotates the loop so the null test sits at the
  bottom with a single copy and the id compare branches backwards to the advance, while mwcc
  emits the unrotated form with the null test duplicated. Not a spelling problem: the residue
  is loop rotation, which 1.2 b56 only performs for a different source shape. Parked until the
  adjacent 0x02029704 / 0x02026350 pair is understood.

  arm9 0x0202612c 20B -- `func_02026140(); func_020261d0();` and nothing else. Retail is
  `stmdb sp!,{lr}` + two `bl` + `ldm sp!,{lr}` + `bx lr` with no frame. mwcc 1.2/b56 emits
  `sub sp, sp, #4` / `add sp, sp, #4` around the calls to keep SP 8-byte aligned (28B), so no
  C spelling can be 20B. Same class as 0x0203b4b0: hand-written assembly thunk, and it also
  explains the 15 remaining sub-28B units, which are all bare `bx r1` veneers, `svc` wrappers,
  multi-register copy loops or `subs`-loop aligners.

  arm9 0x020101f8 80B -- fill loop: `for (j = 0; j < 4; j++) *(u8 *)(*(u32 *)(*(u32 *)((u8 *)&data_02042a68
  + 0x10) + 0x158) + (i << 3) + j + 0x24) = 0x45;` then the same store with 0x17. Right size (80B),
  identical instruction sequence, only register colour differs: retail keeps the pool base in r1
  and the hoisted `i << 3` in r2 with the constant in r3, mwcc 1.2/b56 insists on base r0, constant
  r2, offset r3. Tried locals for the base and for the offset, `(i * 8)`, `(i << 3) + j` grouping,
  do-while, and pointer locals. Register-allocation tie.

  arm9 0x020181b0 88B -- list insert: `h = (u32 *)(p + 0x24); old = h[0]; n = func_020183ac(h, 0x10, 4);
  if (!n) return 0; n[0] = v; n[1] = old; n[2] = h[1]; n[3] = h[2]; h[2] = n; return 1;`. Exactly 88B
  and byte-identical except that retail holds `h` in r4 and `old` in r5 where mwcc holds them the
  other way round. Split declarations, reordered declarations and `*h` vs `h[0]` all keep the swap.

  arm9 0x020202b4 76B -- display-mode swap: read u16 global 0x02048c70, store 1 to 0x0204236c, then
  `if (v == 0) { REG_DISPCNT |= 0x10000; return; } REG_DISPCNT = (REG_DISPCNT & ~0x30000) | (v << 16);`.
  mwcc emits 72B with the second block if-converted (`movne/ldrne/bicne/orrne/strne`); retail
  branches with `bxeq lr` and straight-line else (76B). Inverting the condition, else-form,
  early-return-both-ways, a `volatile` register pointer and a split read/modify/write all keep
  the predicated form.

  arm9 0x0203b698 56B -- walks the NULL-terminated function-pointer array at 0x02042228 calling
  each entry: `stmdb sp!,{lr}; ldr r4,[pc]; b test; loop: blx r0; add r4,r4,#4; test: cmp r4,#0;
  popeq; bxeq; ldr r0,[r4]; cmp r0,#0; bne loop; pop; bx lr`. Retail tests r4 against zero even
  though it is the table address, so the source keeps the pointer in a variable the compiler
  cannot prove non-NULL. Three spellings (array global, pointer global, u32 + cast) all fail.

  arm9 0x0201fd50 60B -- brother of the matched func_0201fd18: DIVCNT/DIV_NUMER/DIV_RESULT
  init with a single argument (`*(volatile u16 *)0x04000280 = 1; p[0] = 0; p[1] = 0x1000;
  q[0] = a0; q[1] = 0;` where p = 0x04000290, q = 0x04000298). Size and instruction sequence
  are exact; retail materialises the constant 1 into r3 and keeps p in r2, mwcc materialises
  1 into r2 and keeps p in r3, and every later reference follows. Five declaration orders
  (p then q, q then p, late q, late p, named u16 pointer), the direct-cast form, dropped
  `volatile` and a hoisted `p[1]` store all keep the swap at 60B / diff 6. Builds 1.2 base,
  b56, sp2p3, sp3, sp4 agree; 2.0.* produce 44B instead. Register-allocation tie in a
  straight-line function, not a control-flow difference.

  ov004 0x0204d980 60B -- Shift-JIS lead byte test: `c = *a0; if (c >= 0x81 && c <= 0x9f)
  return 1; if (c >= 0xe0 && c <= 0xef) return 1; return 0;`. Retail materialises the first
  range as a stored boolean (`mov r2, #0` / `movls r2, r0` / `cmp r2, #0` / `bxne lr`) and
  keeps the second as a direct branch, so the source clearly assigned that half to a
  variable. Five flag spellings (r = 0 then r = 1, ternary, r = expr, `r != 0`, a second
  constant `z = 1`) all fold back to 52B: mwcc re-fuses the flag into the compare. Needs the
  first `&&` result to escape, which the source of a leaf predicate cannot express.

  arm9 0x0202cff8 84B -- two guards then a masked field clear and a call: `if (a0 != 0xb)
  return; if (a2 == 0) return; p = data_0204aec0; *(u32 *)(p + 0x34) &= ~0x20;
  func_02025118(*(u32 *)(p + 0xd0));`. Size, frame and every instruction match; the only
  difference is the pool register. Retail loads the base after both guards into r2 (a2 is
  dead by then); mwcc hoists the same load above the first branch into r3 so it never
  clobbers the live a2. Initialising `p` at its declaration gives that hoist (84B, diff 10);
  leaving `p` uninitialised and assigning it after the guards makes mwcc rematerialise the
  address per use instead (two pool words). `volatile u8 *p` keeps the hoist, `u8 *volatile`
  costs 96B. Same pool-scheduling class as 0x0201fd50.

  ov003 0x0205299c 76B and ov008 0x0204e51c 56B -- both are the DTCM flag write already
  parked five times (`0x027c0000 + 0x3ff8`, retail pools base and offset separately and uses
  `ldr r0,[r1,r0]!` writeback, mwcc folds the sum into one literal). ov003 additionally has
  the indirect call `if (*(void (**)(void))data_ov003_020eb720) (*(...))();` which reproduces
  on its own; only the shared tail diverges. Do not re-spend rounds on these two: they inherit
  whatever breaks the twin set (they are now the 6th and 7th members).

  arm9 0x02008178 96B -- size-table lookup plus a doubler loop: `if (a0 != 0xb) return
  *(s32 *)(data_0203c0f4 + a0 * 12); if (a1 == 0) return 0; v = 0x7d0; i = 1; if (a1 <= 1)
  return v; do { v *= 2; if (v >= 0x3b9ac9ff) return 0x3b9ac9ff; i++; } while (i < a1);
  return v;`. Signedness matters (`ldrge`/`blt`, not `ldrhs`/`blo`), and hoisting `i = 1`
  above the `a1 <= 1` test is what puts `mov r3, #1` before `bxle`. That leaves ONE
  instruction: retail reloads the cap (`ldrge r0, [pc, #0x14]`, same pool word it compared
  against) while mwcc reuses r2 (`movge r0, r2`). Six literal spellings, a local `limit`
  variable, `> limit - 1`, decimal/hex/`L`/`U` suffixes and four loop forms all keep the
  reuse, and 1.2 base/sp2/sp3/sp4 all CSE it, so this is not a build difference. Parked at
  diff 2 (96B both).

  arm9 0x0201c2d4 96B -- `if (!a0) return; if (*(u8 *)(a0 + 0x2c) == 0) return; if (a1 == 0)
  { func_0201c140(); return; } func_0201fc78(a0 + 0x1c, 0); func_0201bf74(a0, 0);
  *(u8 *)(a0 + 0x2c) = 2;`. Retail is 96B with `mov r2, r1` at entry and loads the state byte
  into r1 (`ldrb r1, [r4, #0x2c]`), so a1 was moved out of r1 before the test; mwcc keeps a1
  in r1 and is one instruction short (92B). Copying a1 into a local `flag` before the guards
  does not stop mwcc coalescing the two. Needs a1's live range to end before the byte load,
  which this source shape does not express.

  arm9 0x02028920 and 0x02028958 56B (famsurvey pair) -- `add r0, a0, #0x28; (-0x28) +=
  (+0x34); o = *(a0 + 8); (*(fn **)(o + 0x40 or 0x3c))(o, old28, (+0x2c));`. Retail allocates
  the object pointer to r4 (`push {r4, lr}`); mwcc keeps it in ip/r3 and emits
  `stmdb sp!,{lr}` + `sub sp, #4` instead, so the frame instruction itself differs even
  though the total is 8 bytes either way. Same saved-register tie as 0x0201fd50; both members
  are unmatched, so one solution would land two units.

  arm9 0x02023df8 and 0x02023e58 96B (2x96 family, differ only in 0x6600000 vs
  0x6400000) -- `h = data_02042370; if (h != (u32)-1) func_020268e0(h, a0, 0x6600000 + a1,
  a2); else func_020269ec(a0, 0x6600000 + a1);`. Retail shares the constant in ip
  (`mov ip, #0x6600000` before the branch, then `add r2, ip, r4` / `add r1, ip, r4`) and is
  96B; mwcc re-encodes it as an add-immediate per use (`add r2, r4, #0x6600000`) and lands
  one instruction short at 92B. Sharing it through a local `base`, computing `addr` once
  before the branch, and pointer-typed forms all fold back to the immediate (the `addr`
  version also shrinks the frame to 88B). 0x6600000 is a legal rotated add-immediate, so
  this is a constant-CSE-vs-rematerialise tie. Both members unmatched.

  arm9 0x02033bd0 and 0x02033c1c 60B (2x60 family) -- hand-written assembly, not C:
  `ldr r1, [pc]; bx r1` veneer into inline `mrs r3, apsr / and r1, #0x40 / orreq / bicne /
  msr cpsr_fc / tst / moveq 1 / movne 0` interrupt enable-with-save. Leaves asm.

RESOLVED (2026-11-22): arm9 0x0201f3d0 and 0x0201c2d4 are both matched; see
notes/mwccarm-codegen-log.md 2.83. The missing instruction in each was a call argument
the visible signature did not have: 0x0201f3d0 passes the just-tested bitfield
(`func_0201f38c(a0, b, a1)`) so the `asr` result already sits in r1, and 0x0201c2d4
passes the state byte (`func_0201c140(a0, v, a1)`). The entry `mov r2, r1` in both was
the tell. Any other parked unit whose retail prologue copies an argument register
without an obvious reason should be re-read as a higher-arity call before being
written off.

  arm9 0x0201cae0 116B -- loop over `a0->count` entries, each indexing an 8-byte table
  at data_02047e18 to build an offset for func_02027b4c and passing the result to
  func_02027104. Two things decide it: the bound must be read as `s32` so the guard is
  `bxle` (a separate `if (n == 0) return;` duplicates the test and costs 12B), and the
  loop locals must be declared `u32 id;` before `s32 i;` -- the reverse order puts the
  cursor in r6 and the id in r5 where retail has them the other way round.

  arm9 0x02028920 and 0x02028958 56B (updated) -- the earlier "three allocations missing"
  note is obsolete: the calls are three-argument, `cb(o, *(u32 *)(a0 + 0x2c), *(u32 *)(a0
  + 0x28))`, in that order. With that arity the frame becomes retail's `push {r4, lr}` and the
  unit is size-exact at 56B; what remains is two instructions: retail loads `[a0 + 0x34]`
  before `[lr]` where mwcc loads them the other way round, and the callback lands in `ip`
  for retail and `r3` for us. Reordering the add operands, hoisting the addend into a
  declared-first local, and dropping the local all move other things instead.

  arm9 0x0202cc90 92B -- spin-wait wrapper: `g = data_0204aec0; s = func_02026350(); while
  (*(u32 *)(g + 0x34) & 4) func_020251c0(g + 0xd4); func_02026364(s); return
  *(u32 *)(*(u32 *)g) == 0;`. Size-exact at 92B with `g` declared after `s`; retail hoists
  the pool load above the `bl` and keeps `g` in r6 with `s` in r5, mwcc emits `bl` then the
  load with `g` in r5 and `s` in r6. Declaring/assigning `g` first grows it to 96B.

  arm9 0x0202bda0 120B -- `func_0202bfdc(0, &f)` then `*a0 = (f & 8) != 0; *a1 = (f
  & 4) != 0;` under null guards. Size-exact at 120B; every instruction matches except that
  retail loads the halfword and mirrors the bool into r1 where mwcc uses r0 (r0, mwcc's
  first free register, is the dead result of the failed status call for retail too).

  arm9 0x0202d9f4 88B -- `func_02026e2c(*(u32 *)(g + 0x24), 0x04100010, *(u32 *)(g + 0x1c),
  0x200); v = *(u32 *)(g + 0x18); func_0202da4c((v >> 8) | 0xb7000000, v << 24); *(volatile
  u32 *)0x040001a4 = *(u32 *)(data_0204b4c0 + 4);` with `g = data_0204aec0`. Retail keeps g in
  r4 across the first call (`push {r4, lr}`); mwcc rematerialises the pool word and only needs
  `stmdb sp!,{lr}` + `sub sp,#4`, so we come out 100B against 88B. Declaring g as a local,
  before or after the other declarations, does not stop the rematerialisation.

  arm9 0x020260b8 116B -- hand-written dispatcher (`mrs r1, apsr`, `teq`, banked `sp`
  switch). Not expressible as C; stays delinked assembly.

  arm9 0x020123e0 116B -- `if (a0 > 0xc) return 0;` (so a0 is signed; `u32` yields `movhi`
  instead of retail's `movgt`), then a four-byte loop over `data_0203bf70 + a0 * 8` and a final
  `func_0200ff30(*(u8 *)(data_0203bf74 + a0 * 8)) != 0`. Size-exact at 116B with `s32 a0`.
  Remaining: retail keeps a0 itself in r6 across the loop (`mov r6, r0` before the range check,
  `add r4, r0, r6, lsl #3`) and re-scales it for the last load; mwcc keeps `a0 * 8` in r4
  instead and indexes with `[rX, r4]`. Both spellings tried (pointer walk and `+ i` re-index)
  hold the scaled value.

  arm9 0x0202c018 120B -- slot setup: `func_0202c3e0(a0)` guard, `*(u16 *)(data_0204acdc + a0
  * 8) = 0`, `*(u32 *)(data_0204acb8 + 4) = a2`, `*(u32 *)(data_0204acb8 + 8) = a3`,
  `*(u32 *)(data_0204ace0 + a0 * 8) = a1`, `func_0202bd70((a0 & 0xff) | 0x03006500)`. Size-exact
  at 120B; retail holds the scaled index in lr and the zero in r8, mwcc swaps those two and
  schedules the `orr` before the `strh` instead of after. Hoisting the or'd argument into its
  own local does not change the colouring.
  arm9 0x0202f28c 312B -- match guards (`func_0202e1cc(2, 7, 8)`, `func_02025c5c(p + 0x10, 4)`,
  `*(u32 *)(p + 0x10) == 1 -> 3`), `a2 < func_0202e7ec() -> 6`, `a2 & 0x3f -> 6`,
  `a4 < func_0202e8d8() -> 6`, `a4 & 0x1f -> 6`, then
  `func_0202e418(0xe, a0)` and `func_0202e2fc(0xe, 0xa, a1, a2 >> 1, a3, a4, a5, a6, a7, a8, a9, a10)`.
  Size-exact at 312B; retail puts a0..a3 in r7..r4 and the `func_0202e2ec()` base in r8, mwcc
  puts a0..a3 in r8..r5 and the base in r4. Declaration order (`u8 *h` first, last, or assigned
  after the declarations), `u32` instead of `u8 *`, and a `volatile` base all leave the pair
  swapped; this is a pure colouring tie like the ip/lr families above.

  arm9 0x020208b0 140B and 0x0202093c 140B -- identical twins: `*(u16 *)(data_02048c74 + 0x18) = v`,
  `p[0] = (p[0] | old18) & ~v`, `v == 0 -> *(u32 *)0x04001000 &= ~0x80000000`,
  `v == 0x100 -> |= 0x80000000` plus `*(u8 *)0x04000249 = 0x83`, then
  `func_02021538(*(u16 *)data_02048c74)`. Size-exact at 140B with the branch shape retail has;
  the whole residue is which scratch register holds `~v`: retail `mvn ip, r0` with the old
  `p[0]` in r3, mwcc `mvn r3, r0` with the old `p[0]` in ip. Locals for both halves, operand
  order in the `&`, an explicit `u32 nv = ~v`, `u16` vs `u32` parameter and a
  `~v & (u | t)` spelling all keep the pair swapped.

  arm9 0x02026a88 28B, 0x02026aa4 36B and 0x0202474c 36B -- 12-byte chunk copies
  (`ldm r0!, {r2, r3, ip}` / `stm r1!, {r2, r3, ip}`, the 0x0202474c twin storing without
  writeback). mwcc never emits this shape from a struct assignment: it opens a frame, keeps
  the two pointers in `lr`/`ip` and moves 16 bytes at a time, so a 36-byte copy comes out 52B
  instead of 28B. One struct, three 12-byte members, three separate struct copies and
  `u8 *` chunk casts all produce the same framed shape.

  arm9 0x0202612c 20B and 0x0203b4b0 52B -- bodies that are only calls
  (`func_02026140(); func_020261d0();` and the two `func_0203b6d0`/`func_0203b6dc` zero-fills).
  Retail's prologue is a bare `stmdb sp!, {lr}` with no padding; mwcc always inserts
  `sub sp, sp, #4` to put sp back on an 8-byte boundary before an external call, which costs
  8B in both. Adding a second saved register flips mwcc to `push {r4, lr}` and the padding
  disappears, but then the body no longer matches.

  arm9 0x02024b5c 48B -- `if (a0 >= 0x60) { p++; a0 -= 0x60; } else a0 -= 0x40;` then
  `*p |= 0x80000000 >> a0` on `(u32 *)0x0207ffb0`. Retail tests the threshold with
  `cmp r0, #0x60` + `pl`/`mi` and keeps `mov r1, #0x80000000 / lsr r1, r1, r0` as its own
  pair before the `ldr`/`orr`/`str`. mwcc emits `ge`/`lt` for every spelling tried (including
  `0x60 <= a0` and `a0 - 0x60 >= 0`) and folds the shift into the `orr` operand, so the unit
  lands at 44B.

  ov003 0x020b6b60 52B and 0x020b732c 52B -- three 4-byte stack arrays zeroed with `strh`
  pairs. Size-exact with three `u16 a[2] = {0, 0};` declarations, but retail puts the zero in
  r0 and the array pointers in r1/r2 where mwcc does the reverse. Declaration order of the
  three arrays does not move it.

  arm9 0x020048e8 20B and 0x020269ec 24B -- fill/copy loops `while (p < end)` with a single
  store as the body. Retail if-converts the body at the top of the loop (`add ip, r1, r2` /
  `cmp r1, ip` / `stmlt r1!, {r0}` / `blt` back to the cmp) and keeps the end pointer in ip;
  mwcc rotates the loop into an early-exit guard plus a bottom test (+8B) whatever the
  spelling (while, for with the increment in the header, separate store and bump, u8 cursor).
  Pointer type and end-expression associativity do not move it.

  arm9 0x020263c0 12B -- `while (n >= 4) n -= 4;`. Retail is `subs r0, r0, #4` / `bhs`, i.e.
  it folds the decrement's borrow into the branch. mwcc pre-tests in every spelling
  (`cmp r0,#4` / `bxlo lr` then a rotated body); the do-while spelling drops the fold and
  emits `sub` / `cmp` / `bhs` at 16B.

  arm9 0x0202c430 24B -- idle wait on a global (`while (data == 1);`). Size-exact at 24B in
  all spellings; retail loads the address into ip (`ldr ip, [pc]` / `ldr r0, [ip]`) where
  mwcc loads it into r0 and immediately clobbers it (`ldr r0, [pc]` / `ldr r0, [r0]`).
  Pointer local, array form, volatile cast and a do-while with a copy all land the same.

  arm9 0x0202497c 76B -- 12-byte record triple store (two call-free stores plus a `& mask`
  result). Retail keeps the byte offset (r6), the shift amount (r4) and the constant 1 (r5)
  live across the call; mwcc keeps only the index and re-derives `index + 8`, so it saves two
  registers instead of three and pads the frame by 4B (72B result).

  arm9 0x02032be8 80B -- chunked callback writer. Same 80B total but retail emits `b test`

  and do-while spellings all keep mwcc's layout.

  arm9 0x020266c0 80B -- hardware register read-modify-write through a computed pointer.
  Size-exact at 80B; retail homes the constants in r1 and the pointer in r2 where mwcc homes
  them in r0 and r1. volatile pointer spelling and a named local do not move the colouring.

  arm9 0x0202d158 212B and 0x0202d22c 212B -- six-argument init with a flag wait loop and a

  ov005 0x020614a4, 0x020656d8, 0x020695bc, 0x0206bfc0 -- four 220B copies of the same init

  arm9 0x0201fd50 60B -- five hardware-register stores at 0x04000280 and the 0x04000290 /

  arm9 0x02026ac8 44B -- 64-byte struct copy. Retail copies in place with 3-register

  arm9 0x0203b4e4 40B -- `*p = (old & ~a0) ^ a1` through a returned pointer. Retail preserves

  arm9 0x020202b4 76B -- two-word status write then a DISPCNT update chosen by a stored

  mwcc instead if-converts the else block on `ne`, for the if/else spelling, the

  arm9 0x0202cff8 84B -- guarded flag clear plus one call. Retail loads the base once

  arm9 0x02023df8 96B (and 0x02023e58, base 0x06400000) -- dispatch on a -1 sentinel into a

  drops the `mov`.
  arm9 0x02027080 88B -- packs nine incoming words into two command words and passes them
  as the 4th and 5th arguments of a call. A single OR tree compiles to 80B (mwcc folds the `lsl`
  into the `orr` operands); split assignments per sub-word (`w1 = (a3 << 26) | (a1 << 24);
  w1 |= a9 << 16; w1 = a4 | w1;` and the same for `w2`) give retail's size exactly at 88B, and
  then only the interleaving of the two chains and the temporaries' registers differ. Reordering
  the two chains, or building `w2` first, changes the frame or keeps the same residue.
  arm9 0x0201780c 116B -- insert counterpart of the 0x0201779c removal just matched (same
  `*(u16 *)(list + 0xa)` key, same `u16 *cnt` pointer for the counter, same two tail calls).
  Size and instruction order are identical, but retail keeps the copied link in ip and the
  `n + k` destination in lr while mwcc swaps them (`ldr ip,[r1,r4]` / `add lr,r2,r4` vs
  `ldr lr,[r1,r4]` / `add ip,r2,r4`), which is five differing words in the linked ROM. Naming
  the destination as a `u32 *`, dropping the `first` variable, and reordering the declarations
  all keep the swap. `reg.py` reports miss, so the unit stays parked with the exact residue.
  arm9 0x02027f74 116B -- lock, store a slot in a table, set/clear a bit in 0x027ffc00+0x388,
  unlock. 108B: the table store needs `extern u32 data_0204ab68[]` (a literal base expands to
  lsl/add/add/str, four instructions), and the two `add r3, r2, #0x388` sites retail materialises
  are folded into the pool constant by mwcc for every spelling tried (`u32 base = 0x027ffc00`,
  `(u32 *)0x027ffc00 + 0xe2`, a pad-struct member, a shared pointer variable). Everything else
  matches, including the `mov r1, #1` / `orr r1, r2, r1, lsl r4` pair.
  arm9 0x02034594 116B -- init a {buf, n, 0} descriptor, hand it to func_0203465c with the
  callback pointer 0x02034608, then force NUL termination. Size and instruction structure match;
  the residue is three register names: retail keeps the descriptor's zero word in ip and the
  `p[-1]` tail in r1/r2, mwcc gives us r2 and r0/r1. `char *p = buf + n; p[-1] = 0;` is required
  to get `addne r1, r5, r4` / `strbne ..., [r1, #-1]` (plain `buf[n - 1] = 0;` yields subne plus an
  indexed store), and the struct must cover all three words so the stores stay sp/sp+4/sp+8.
  arm9 0x020366bc 116B -- bounded copy that tests the byte it just stored (retail reloads it
  through the pre-increment destination address in ip, then zero-fills the remainder). 72B: every

  `volatile u8 *dst` so the reload cannot be folded) makes mwcc merge the four early returns into
  one epilogue; retail duplicates the epilogue at each of the four exits, which is most of the
  44-byte gap. The readback is not evidence of a volatile destination: the volatile form compiles
  to the same 72B.

  arm9 0x020340d8 112B -- scan three 0x4c-byte records from data_02042690: for each, test a 3-bit
