  otherwise. The closest spelling reaches 144B: retail accumulates the row base in one register and forms
  `add r0, r2, ip` inside the inner loop, while mwcc recomputes `add r1, r4, r2` from the global base and
  then increments that address, adding one instruction. Giving the row base its own advancing pointer
  instead makes it worse (176B) because mwcc then materialises both bases. Casting the stored `i` to u8
  makes no difference; retail's strb comes from a plain u32 store to a u8 lvalue.
  arm9 micro-stubs (checked once, all asm-shaped, do not retry as C): 0x02033cb0 8B is `udf #0xffff;
  bx lr`; 0x0203b2cc 8B is `cmp r1, #0; bxeq lr` and falls through into the next unit when r1 != 0;
  0x02024af8 12B is `ldr r1, [pc]; bx r1`, a thunk to a fixed target (pool 0x02024b04); 0x0203b564 and
  0x0203b578 20B each and 0x0203b58c 16B are BIOS `svc #0x123456` wrappers with the call number in r0.
  None of these has a C spelling that keeps the fall-through, the udf or the svc.
  (addendum, 0x0205fe04) the reason no spelling reaches retail's shape: mwcc always fuses
  `b + j*3 + i` into one accumulating address register, so its inner body has no per-iteration
  `add r0, rowbase, i`. Giving the row base a pointer of its own makes mwcc keep two live bases and
  unroll the 2-trip outer loop (176B). Both branches are recorded in notes/mwccarm-codegen.md 3.13.
  arm9 0x02032c70 144B -- 16-bit ring/cursor update: `arr = *(u32 *)(0x027fff60 + 0x1c) + 0x8000`;
  `h = p[0]`; `idx = (h + 1) & 0x3fff`; return when `idx == p[2]`; else merge one byte of the u16 argument
  `v` into `arr[h & ~1]` -- the high byte when `h` is odd (`(old & 0xff) | (v << 8)`), the low byte when
  it is even (`(old & 0xff00) | v`) -- and store `p[0] = idx`. Pools resolved: 0x027fff60 and the 0x3fff
  index mask. Spelling with plain masks reaches 124B against 144B: retail converts through `lsl #0x10` +
  `lsr #0x10` pairs (three of them across the two paths) where the masks fold them away, and adding
  explicit `(u16)` casts on the stored expressions drops it to 112B. The 20-byte gap is exactly those
  conversion pairs, so the source needs a shape that makes mwcc re-extend the u16 argument at each use
  rather than keeping it in a register.
  (update, 0x02032c70) best spelling so far is 136B against 144B: keep the merged value in a u16
  local and the extended argument in a u32 local assigned from the u16 parameter, then store the local.
  Two instructions are still missing versus retail, which extends the argument separately inside each
  branch instead of hoisting it once.
  (update, 0x02032c70) best spellings are 136B and 140B against 144B. Two levers found: writing the
  global as `typedef struct { u8 pad[0x1c]; u32 p; } T;` and loading `((T *)0x027fff60)->p` reproduces
  retail's pooled base plus `#0x1c` displacement (an absolute `0x027fff60 + 0x1c` folds to one literal),
  and `u16 idx = (u16)(h + 1) & 0x3fff;` (cast on the sum, not on the result) makes mwcc emit retail's
  `lsl #0x10`/`lsr #0x10` truncation pair but masks in the wrong order, reaching 140B. With the cast
  moved to the assignment (`u16 idx = (h + 1) & 0x3fff;`, or via a `u32 m = 0x3fff;` local) mwcc folds
  the pair away and it drops to 136B. Retail has both the pair and the and-before-truncate order, which
  no spelling tried has produced together.
  (retest, 0x0205299c and the 0x027c0000 family) the typed-struct lever from playbook 3.14 does NOT
  apply here: `typedef struct { u8 pad[0x3ff8]; u32 v; } T; ((T *)0x027c0000)->v |= 1;` still folds to
  the single 0x027c3ff8 literal (68B against 76B), because mwcc constant-folds a struct field off a
  constant base pointer. Section 3.14's split only survives when the field offset is small enough to
  remain a real displacement from a pooled base.
  family survey note (2026-11-22): ov002 has 58 identical-skeleton groups and the value is concentrated
  in a few: 13x756 (0x0208f860, 0x0209a5f4, 0x0209c728, 0x0209eb18, 0x020a8ba0, 0x020ad2b0, ...) worth
  9,828B, 14x608 from 0x0208f38c worth 8,512B, 10x688 from 0x0209ab8c worth 6,880B, 10x676 from
  0x0208f0e8 worth 6,760B. A representative, 0x0208f860, is not a quick win: it opens with a nine-register
  push, a 0x44 stack frame, `mul r2, r0, #0x158` for a record stride and at least four calls in the first
  80 bytes (0x0205bf64, 0x02034148, 0x02053468, 0x0205bee4), so it needs structured reconstruction rather
  than a shape guess. The small-function levers in playbook 3.13/3.14 do not transfer.
  ov002 0x0207ff34 116B -- per-index byte counter with a clamp: `b = *(u32 *)0x02102070`; `p = b + 0x3654`;
  when `*(s32 *)(p + a0*4 + 0x40)` is not positive it loads `v = *(u8 *)(p + 0x30 + a0)`, adds a1, forces 3
  when `*(u8 *)(b + 0x8d) == 2`, clamps to 0..3, and stores back. A spelling with `u8 *q = p + 0x30` and
  `q[a0]` for both accesses reaches 112B against 116B: it is one `add` short because retail materialises
  `q = p + 0x30` before the early-return compare and the store address `q + a0` in a register alongside
  it, while mwcc defers both and recomputes `q + a0` for the store. Introducing an explicit `u8 *e =
  q + a0` for the store collapses the unit to 88B because mwcc folds it back into `q[a0]`.
  arm9 0x02026af4 148B (twin 0x02026cc0) -- this is mwcc's inlined byte-fill (memset) expansion, not
  ordinary hand-written C: `cmp r2,#0; bxeq lr` guard, a one-byte head via `tst r0,#1` with an `ldrh/and
  #0xff/orr/strh` merge at [r0,#-1], `orr r1,r1,r1,lsl #8` then `orr r1,r1,r1,lsl #16` to widen the fill
  byte, an aligned `str r1,[r0],#4` loop against `add ip, r3, r0`, a conditional `strhne` tail and a final
  merged byte at `[r0]`. Reconstructing this by hand is the wrong approach; the lever is the opposite
  direction -- any *other* unmatched function that contains this skeleton is one that calls `memset(p, v,
  n)` in source with -inline on, so search for the pattern inside larger units rather than rewriting the
  runtime routine.
  arm9 0x0201ff64 156B -- fixed-point cross product with rounding, source recovered exactly:
  `out[0] = (s32)(((s64)a[1]*b[2] - (s64)a[2]*b[1] + 0x800) >> 12)` and the two cyclic permutations,
  with all six inputs copied to s32 locals first. Writing the loads as locals is what makes it size-exact:
  indexing `a[...]`/`b[...]` inline gives 180B because mwcc re-loads between stores, while the six locals
  reach 156B, the same size as retail with 28 of 39 instructions matching. The 11 differing instructions
  are pure register choice and load order: retail ends up with a1 in sb, b2 in ip, a2 in r0, b1 in fp,
  a0 in r3 and b0 in sl, mine puts a2 in lr, b1 in sl, b0 in fp. Swapping the declaration order of the a
  and b locals and adding a u64 cast on the shifted value both leave the size and the 11-line residue
  unchanged. Casting to u64 does match retail's `lsr`+`orr` shift form either way.
  (exhausted, 0x0201ff64) permute.py closed both scheduling axes: all 6 orders of the three
  output statements score 148 (worse than the 156 baseline), and all 720 orders of the six input
  declarations score exactly 156 -- mwcc ignores declaration order when choosing load order and
  registers here. The s64-temp formulation (`x = (s64)a1*b2 - (s64)a2*b1;` then `out[0] = (s32)((x +
  0x800) >> 12)`) also scores 156 with the same 11-instruction residue. So the remaining difference is
  not statement order, declaration order or formulation; it is an allocator decision with no source-level
  handle found. Do not re-run permutations on this unit.
  (permute, 0x0207ff34) permuting the six body statements (720 orders) never reaches a match: the
  best orders all score 40 on permute's metric and the ones it ranks first move the load and the store
  apart from their uses, so they are not semantically valid even though they compile. The unit stays at
  112B against 116B. Same for the ordering axes on 0x0201ff64, which are already closed.
  ov002 0x0205bee4 128B -- movement-step scaler, source recovered: `g = *(u8 **)data_ov002_02102070`;
  `t = g + 0x274 + *(u8 *)(a1 + 2) * 0x1c`; `src = 0` when `*(u8 *)(a1 + 0xd1) == 0` and
  `((*(u8 *)(t + 2) + 0xd9) & 0xff) <= 1`, else `src = *(u16 *)(a1 + 0xe8)`; then
  `n = (src & 0xffff) >> 1`, `if (n > 2) n = 2`, `return (s32)(a0 * (2 - n)) / 2;`. That spelling is
  exactly 128B with only two residues: mwcc if-converts the src selection (`movls r2,#0; bls`) where
  retail branches (`mov r1,#0; b` then `ldrh r1,[r1,#0xe8]`), and mwcc masks the u16 with a pooled
  `and` where retail truncates with `lsl #0xf`/`lsr #0x10`. Writing the selection as
  `src = 0; if (!(cond)) src = ...` or casting `(u32)(u16)src` instead of masking drops the unit to
  120B, so both of those moves lose the branch retail keeps. Parked at 128B.
  (update, 0x0205bee4) three more truncation forms measured, all worse than the 128B baseline:
  `n = (u32)((u16)src) / 2;` 120B, `n = ((u32)((u16)src) << 15) >> 16;` 120B,
  `n = ((u16)src) >> 1;` 124B. The `(src & 0xffff) >> 1` form stays the best at 128B.
  (update, 0x0205bee4) the nested form `if (a1[0xd1] != 0) src = 0; else if (x <= 1) src = 0; else src = ldrh;` is also exactly 128B but with 18 differing instructions, versus 8 for the plain if/else, so the plain form remains the closest.
  (update, 0x0205bee4) slots.py shows the best candidate puts src in r2 and n in r1 while retail uses r1 for both, so the source likely reuses one variable; collapsing src and n into a single u32 v gives 128B with 15 differing instructions (worse than the 13 of the two-variable form). Declaring the value u16 gives 116B. sweep.py over truncation/clamp/return (16 combinations) and over the selection statement (4) found no match; best remains the plain if/else two-variable form at 128B.
  arm9 0x02028920 56B (twin 0x02028958, differing only in the vtable slot 0x40 vs 0x3c) -- o->f28 += o->f34 then a virtual call through o->f8 with args (o->f8, o->f2c, old o->f28); retail loads the old f28 twice. Best spelling 60B with the right `add lr, r0, #0x28` shape but a 4-byte alignment frame and ip instead of r4 for the object; retail has no frame and keeps the object in r4. Inline-expression form is 56B but loses the lr address and the r4 save. scan.py scores this unit 0, so it is still the best small target.
  arm9 0x02024b5c 48B -- sets one bit of a register pair: base 0x027fffb0, and for a0 >= 0x60 it moves to the next word and subtracts 0x60, else subtracts 0x40, then ORs 0x80000000 >> a0. Parameter must be s32 (u32 gives addhs/sublo where retail has addpl/submi); with s32 mwcc emits addge and drops one instruction, 44B against 48. Retail predicated pl/mi comes from a comparison mwcc could prove overflow-free, which the plain if/else does not give it.
  arm9 0x0203b4e4 40B -- `p = func_0203b6d0(); old = *p; *p = (old & ~a0) ^ a1; return old;`. Retail preserves the arguments by pushing r0-r2 plus ip (5-register push/pop) and emits a single `bic ip, r2, r0`; mwcc moves the arguments into r4/r5 with a frame and emits `mvn`+`and` instead, 56B against 40B. The argument-preservation form is what differs: retail keeps the args in their incoming registers across the call.
  arm9 0x020255e8 100B -- initialises a 0x48-byte record: [0x40] = a1+4, [0x44] = a2, [0x38] = a2-0x40, [0x00] = (a1+4) \& 1 ? 0x3f : 0x1f, then [0x04]..[0x34] and [0x3c] = 0. Exact 100B in every spelling tried (incrementing the parameters in place, s32 params, inline a1+4 twice, u8* against struct fields); the only difference is allocation: retail adds into r1 and subtracts into r2 in place, mwcc adds into r3 and subtracts into r1. reg.py does not accept it as a renaming, so this is an allocator tie-break, not a formulation error.
  arm9 0x0202db70 164B -- page-chunked copy from a global stream state at 0x0204aec0: [0x18] = position, [0x1c] = source cursor, [0x20] = bytes left. If (position & ~0x1ff) == a0[8], copy min(0x200 - (position & 0x1ff), left) from the cursor to a0 + 0x20 + offset, advance [0x18] and [0x1c], decrement [0x20]; returns [0x20] != 0. Seven spellings tried; four reach exactly 164B but never retail's allocation. Retail loads the base once into r5 and keeps it live across the bl (add r1, r5, #0x18 after the call), re-reads [r5+0x18] instead of reusing the masked value, and applies movhi from a loaded value. mwcc always rematerialises the 0x0204aec0 literal from the pool after the call and CSEs the second position read; marking the pointer volatile fixes the size but turns movhi into ldrhi. This is the documented address-materialisation tie.
  arm9 0x020208b0 140B -- video-state selector on the u16 pair at 0x02048c74 (fields 0 and 0xc): prev = s[0xc]; old = s[0]; s[0xc] = a0; s[0] = (old | prev) & ~a0; then if a0 == 0 clear bit 31 of 0x04001000, else if a0 == 0x100 set it and store 0x83 to 0x04000249; tail calls func_02021538(s[0]). Control flow needs the inverted form `if (a0 != 0) { if (a0 == 0x100) ... } else ...`: the natural `if (a0 == 0) ... else if ...` makes mwcc if-convert the clear into ldreq/biceq/streq and lands at 136B, and the 0x100-first order also gives 136B. Nine spellings (u16*/u8*/u32* pointer, value in a temp, volatile stores, both declaration orders) all reach exactly 140B with the branch layout matching the retail beq to 0x2020908; what is left is 8 instructions in the prologue, all register names plus one scheduling swap: retail keeps the constant pointer in r1 and the mask in ip and stores s[0xc] before the orr, mwcc always puts the pointer in ip, the mask in r2 and hoists the orr above the store. Twin of 0x0202093c in the same 2x140 famsurvey group.
  arm9 0x0202d158 212B -- two-step page/stream submit: g = data_0204aec0 (a real bss symbol, see below), h = func_02026350(), spin-wait on g->f34 bit 2 calling func_020251c0(&g->fd4), set the bit, func_02026364(h), store a1/a0/a2/a3/a4 into g->f18/f1c/f20/f28/f2c, and if the sixth argument is nonzero register the callback 0x0202d300 via func_020cf10 and return 1; otherwise g->fd0 = [0x02048d3c+8], call func_0202d300(g) and return g->f00 == 0. Body is right in every spelling (literal address, extern symbol, struct member access) and all are exactly 212B, but never the same registers: retail holds the base in r6 and the four saved arguments in sl/sb/r8/r7, mwcc 1.2/b56 puts the base in sl and shifts the arguments one register down, and it rematerialises the base from the pool after each call where retail adds to r6.
  NOTE: data_0204aec0 IS declared in config/ATOJ/arm9/symbols.txt (kind:bss). Greps for it must use the full 8-digit form 0x0204aec0; the file writes addr:0x0204aec0 and a search for 0x204aec0 matches nothing. Using the symbol instead of the literal changes the codegen: the base is loaded early into a callee-saved register like retail, but the pointer-variable and struct-member forms then cost an extra mov (208B) and the object form shifts the whole register set (212B). For 0x0202db70 the symbol form compiles to 144B, well under retail's 164B.
  ov002 0x02084ff4 164B -- body matches instruction for instruction except the prologue allocation:
  retail keeps g in r0 and the zero in r1 (`ldr r0,=data_ov002_02102070; mov r1,#0; ldr r3,[r0]`), mwcc puts g in
  r1 and the zero in r0 whether the zero comes from `g[0x64]=0`, a shared `i=0`, or a chained
  `g[0x64]=g[0x65]=0`. Everything from `strb` on is exact.
  ov002 0x0208804c 164B -- 5-byte block swap. Scalar locals collapse to 132B (bytes stay in registers);
  `u8 tmp[5]` with explicit `tmp[j]=g[..]` also collapses; `volatile u8 tmp[5]` restores the stack frame but
  emits load/store pairs one statement at a time, where retail batches two loads then two stores
  (`ldrb r3,[r0,#0x200]; ldrb r2,[r0,#0x201]; strb r3,[sp]; strb r2,[sp,#1]`). Byte-for-byte copy loops
  with constant bounds are not unrolled by mwcc, so the batching shape has no source spelling yet.
  ov004 0x0204e1a0 164B -- found-record setter. Retail keeps two induction variables (`r8 = arr+i*0x1c0`,
  `r7 = i*0x1c0`) and a wider frame (7 registers + 4 bytes); one-pointer and two-pointer spellings both
  compile to 152/184B with different register choices. The `n<=0` early return is already `movle/popeq`.
  arm9 0x020344b4 172B -- memset. Exact at 172B except the 32-byte-block counter lands in r1 where retail
  has r3 (`lsrs r3,r2,#5` / `subs r3,r3,#1`). Reusing the alignment temp, `for`-init, separate locals and
  signed/unsigned variants all keep r1; retail's r3 is the alignment temp's register reused.
  arm9 0x02025e48 172B -- switch on 0..6 returning constants, with a case that loads a pooled literal 0 into
  r1, tests it (`cmp r1,#0; bne`) and then uses it in `subge r0,r2,r1`. The zero literal needs a spelling
  that makes mwcc load a pooled zero instead of `mov`; not attempted past the transcription.
  ov003 0x02082220 96B (twin 0x020c806c) -- minute/second scaler. All three if/else arms assign the same
  constants (0x1e, 0x3c), yet retail keeps a real branch for the first arm and predication for the second
  and third; the equivalent source collapses to one cmp with two predicated pairs (80B). The branch only
  survives if the first arm's test is not the same block-level compare the optimiser can merge.
  ov003 0x020b6b60 52B (twin 0x020b732c) -- six u16 stack slots zeroed through three pointer bases and
  then discarded. Plain source is eliminated outright (4B); `volatile u16` keeps the stores but bases
  every one on sp (40B) instead of the `add rX,sp,#0/4/8` re-basing retail uses, which needs a spelling
  where each pair is reached through its own register.
  ov005 0x020614a4 220B (family 0x020656d8, 0x020695bc, 0x0206bfc0 -- 4x220) -- block init: two GX calls,
  a masked MMIO write, a 0x200-iteration u16 clear with a word store every fourth entry, then a 0x1f4
  guard around a clear/fill pair. Every instruction matches at exactly 220B except the two dead loop
  temps: retail materialises the stored zero in lr and `g + i*2` in ip, mwcc insists on the opposite
  (`mov ip,r5` / `add lr,r4,r5,lsl #1`). The store address only split as `add r0,ip,#0x4a00` +
  `[r0,#0x58]` once the base was a per-iteration `u8 *e = g + i * 2`; the second tail call also has to
  keep its argument as the compound `(u8 *)g + 0x4658` or mwcc reuses r4 for both calls. Tried: u16/u32
  base, `&g[i*2]`, u32 pointer, `e[0x252c]`, `0u`, `i & 0`, a zero variable live across the loop,
  reversed if/else and both store-address groupings -- all land at the same lr/ip swap.
  ov002 0x02089f68 204B -- two-slot jump-table walk. Table is 14 entries at 0x40: case 0 and out-of-range
  go to `e[0x34d+i] = 0`, cases 1/8/9 to `e[0x34f+i] = 0`, cases 2-7/10-13 test `g[0x109] != g[0x10a]`
  before clearing 0x34f; after the switch the same compare clears 0x34b and 0x34d is cleared again, so
  retail really does store 0x34d twice (r3 then r2, both zero). Retail hoists four separate zero
  constants into r3/r2/ip/lr before the loop and uses only 5 registers; the transcription above compiles
  to 236B and 8 saved registers, so the loop body shape (probably a `u32 *`/struct walk rather than byte
  indices) is still wrong.
  data runs -- carve_data.py runs <mod> carves the unclaimed space between claimed units from its word-aligned
  interior. ov008 took 18/18 candidates (372B) and ov006 took 2/10 (564B); the 8 remaining ov006 runs, which
  are 4-8KB each, all change the digest, so size rather than alignment is the next thing to explain. A range
  whose start is not word aligned cannot be carved at all: 0x02040505 2114B was retested and still fails, so
  the filter in accept() is correct.
  data runs, second pass -- the large runs failed to link because a run contains several data symbols from
  symbols.txt and the unit only defined the first (`undefined symbol`, referenced from a neighbour unit).
  runs() now emits every symbol inside the run as its own array in the same unit, which fixes the link, but
  the large runs then link and still change the digest (ov006 0x02076024, 7168B, 2 parts -> 8fcd8eec vs
  9eec3248). So the remaining cause is a byte difference, not definition or placement; bisecting those bytes
  against retail for one run is the next step.
  data runs, resolved -- the large runs differ because a symbol inside them sits at an odd address
  (ov006 0x02076024..0x0207c024 contains data_ov006_0207623b at 0x0207623b). Built overlay vs
  extract/ATOJ/arm9_overlays/ov006.bin: 1665 bytes differ over 109 runs, each record gaining a leading 00 -
  the linker pads around the unaligned symbol and the data shifts by one byte. runs() now skips any run
  containing a symbol whose address or span is not word aligned; what remains links and is byte exact.
  remaining data runs -- after the aligned-stretch split, what is left is packed string tables: in every ov006
  gap between claimed units the symbols are odd-addressed and odd-length with no aligned interior at all
  (0x0205025c span 169, 0x02050305 span 253, 0x02050505 span 343, ...). runs() now carves the aligned
  stretches on either side of an unaligned symbol instead of dropping the run; that is what took ov001's
  4328-byte stretch. Reaching the string tables needs each string as its own sized unit, which is a data
  modelling pass rather than a carve.
  string tables -- a unit placed at an odd address does not just shift its own bytes: carving ov006
  0x0205025c (169B) leaves 122,543 bytes different, the first at 0x0204db14 in .text, where a literal pool
  entry reads 0x02076028 instead of retail's 0x02076024, so the linker rebases around the odd section start.
  That rules out carrying string tables as address-placed units one symbol at a time; they need the owning
  aggregate unit reconstructed as a whole, with the strings laid out inside one section that starts on a
  word boundary. Recorded so the next pass does not re-run this experiment.
  data runs, fourth pass -- the last word-aligned gaps are small and mostly hostile: arm9 3/3 and
  ov004 6/6 and ov002 1/1 all change the digest (arm9 02042354/020423dc/02042a24, ov004 020637c8..020647c0,
  ov002 020fdc94), while ov008 2/2 (0204f27c 32B rodata, 0204f414 44B data) are byte exact and were kept.
  Symbolising the newly carved data units resolved 1,370 more relocation words (arm9 142, ov004 460,
  ov008 9, ov002 759) and left the digest OK. Data 612,772 -> 612,848 B. Every module now reports 0 runs at
  min 16, so the next data work is the string tables, not more carving.
  whole-gap units -- carve_data.py gaps <mod> emits each entire unclaimed gap as one unit, every data
  symbol inside it as a consecutive array, so the strings keep their retail offsets in a section that
  starts and ends word aligned. All 8 ov006 gaps (36,452B) link but change the digest. Measured on
  0x02062024 (3908B, parts 321/32/3555): mwcc word aligns each array, so the third starts at 356 not 353,
  the unit grows by 4 bytes and dsd emits it 32 bytes late, which shifts every pointer into it (first
  difference 0x0204db14 reads 0x02076028 for retail's 0x02076024). The route needs one symbol at offset 0
  and the interior names placed by the delinker rather than by separate C objects, so it is a dsd/symbol
  question, not a byte question.

  segmented table carving -- the packed string tables were carveable after all. Two facts decide it: the
  linker pads every section to 4, so a unit must start and end word aligned; and a section may end inside a
  string only if no still-unowned reference needs a symbol there. So each aligned gap is cut at the interior
  symbols that delinked gap objects (assembly we do not own) or function units still need; every remaining
  stretch becomes one array of exactly its retail bytes, with no per-array alignment that could pad it.
  Interior names used only by carved data units have their `(u32)&name` replaced by the plain address, which
  keeps the bytes and removes the undefined symbol. dsd also infers a gap object's last symbol as running to
  the next symbol, so a boundary must be a symbols.txt address or configure fails with a range error.
  Relocation targets must be attributed through the relocs `module:` field, not the address: overlays load
  over one another, so their address ranges overlap. A missed reference is silent -- mwldarm resolves the
  undefined symbol to 0 (measured: an arm9 table pointing at ov001 strings zeroed 6 words).
  Measured: 16 units kept, 49,636 B (ov005 9/29,068, ov006 4/19,232, ov003 2/1,276, ov002 2/44, ov004 1/16),
  data 612,848 -> 630,724 B. ov001 0x0204ff10 (2,968 B) is blocked: a delinked arm9 rodata object references
  0x02050104 and friends there, so none of those names can disappear.

  thumb swi thunks -- the BIOS wrappers (Halt, Sqrt, IsDebugger, CpuSet, GetCRC16,
  CpuFastSet, RLUnCompReadNormalWrite8bit, LZ77UnCompReadByCallbackWrite16bit, VBlankIntrWait, Mod)
  at 0x020040e6..0x0200470a are `swi 0xNN` + `bx lr` and the source form is the same one the matched
  ones use (`#pragma thumb on`, `asm { swi 0x06 }`), so var3 reports MATCH for all ten. They cannot be
  placed: every one starts or ends at 2 mod 4, and mwldarm pads each section to 4, so carving one splits
  the surrounding gap object at an odd address and the linker inserts 2 bytes of padding before the unit.
  Measured on 0x020040e6: the built arm9.bin holds 00 00 06 df 70 47 where retail has 06 df 70 47, and
  that shift moves the rest of the gap (34281 differing runs up to 0x03e788). A thumb unit is placeable
  only if both its start and its end are word aligned, which none of the thunks are.

  code ties this pass -- arm9 0x0202c430 wants the global address in ip (ldr ip,=data; ldr r0,[ip] loop)
  where 1.2/b56 always picks r0/r1, for every shape tried (while/do-while, volatile, pointer local,
  array, symbol vs literal, u16/u32, extra parameters); a register-allocation tie. ov003 0x020b6b60 (three
  stack u16[2] zero initialisers) matches instruction count and offsets but schedules the base pointers
  r0/r2/r1 where retail uses r1/r2/r1, for every declaration and initialiser form tried -- same class.

  ties found while cloning -- arm9 0x020124e0 / ov004 0x0206256c (68B, `p[a0/8] & (1 << (a0%8))` behind
  a null-checked func_0200faa8()): size and every other slot match, but 1.2/b56 emits the byte load at 0x2c
  and the `% 8` chain at 0x30 where retail has them the other way round; operand order, named temporaries
  and computing the mask first all leave the schedule unchanged. arm9 0x020208b0 / 0x0202093c (140B,
  `data_02048c74[0] = ~a0 & (data[0] | data[0xc])` plus a 0x04001000 enable bit): the inverted-
  if form (`if (a0 != 0) { if (a0 == 0x100) ... } else ...`) reproduces retail's two-branch layout and the
  full 140 bytes. SOLVED 2026-11-23: the remaining ip/r3 swap was the store order; writing the old-value
  store (`data[0] = (data[0] | data[0xc]) & ~a0;`) before the new-value store (`data[0xc] = a0;`) gives
  the retail allocation, and both units are matched in `src/arm9/`.

## 2026-11-23 (later session): register-tie walls

- `arm9 0x02066a54` / `0x020b85b8` / `0x020db6ec` (180 B, 3x). The whole body reproduces
  instruction for instruction with `if (a0 == 0) { ... } else { ... }` (the inverted
  `if (a0 != 0)` either puts the small block first or grows the unit), `u8 *q = base + i*2;`
  and `(i % 4) == 0` — note `i & 3` compiles to `ands` while `i % 4` gives retail's
  `lsr #31 / rsb / adds ... ror #30` sequence. Only an `ip`/`lr` swap is left: retail keeps
  the zero constant in `lr` and the row pointer in `ip`, mwcc always picks the reverse,
  whether the zero is a literal, a `u16`, or a `u32` variable, and whether `q` is declared
  inside the loop, outside it, or spelled inline.
- `arm9 0x0202d158` / `0x0202d22c` (212 B, 2x). Size, field stores, call order and argument
  shuffling all reproduce. Two residues: mwcc emits `mov r1, r6 / ldr r0, [r1, #0x34]`
  where retail has a single `ldr r1, [r6, #0x34]`, and the `0xd0` store reuses `r6` where
  retail rematerialises `data_0204aec0` into `r1`; writing that store through the symbol
  instead fixes the store but then costs a fifth instruction and the unit grows to 216 B.
- `arm9 0x02028920` / `0x02028958` (56 B, 2x). Retail pushes `{r4, lr}` and keeps the target
  object in `r4` across the indirect call; mwcc never allocates `r4` here (it emits
  `stmdb sp!, {lr}; sub sp, sp, #4`) and keeps the object in `ip`. Same instructions, same
  order, different callee-saved choice, unchanged by `register`, by moving the base into a
  variable, or by reading the field twice.
- `arm9 0x02036730` (216 B): hand-optimised word `strcpy` (`0x01010101`/`0x80808080`, first
  word tested before it is stored). No matched analogue in `src/`; not attempted.
## 2026-11-23 (third session): ov003 dead ends around the audio twins

- `ov003 0x020d72f8` / `0x020d9f2c` (164 B, 2x). The body reproduces at exactly 164 B with
  `if (a0 == 0) { ... } return 0;` (the early-return spelling if-converts to
  `addne sp / movne r0 / popne / bxne` and grows to 176 B), `u8 *q = base + i * 2;` and
  `(i % 4) == 0`. Only an `ip`/`lr` swap is left: retail keeps the zero constant in `lr`
  and the row pointer in `ip`, mwcc always picks the reverse. Unchanged by addressing the
  row as `u8 *`, `u32`, `&base[i * 2]` or a second `u8 *r = q + 0x8de8`, by `volatile` on
  the halfword store, by `while` instead of `for`, by writing `i % 4 != 0 {} else {}`, and
  by `(u32)i` / `(u32)base` casts (all 164 B, same swap).
- `ov003 0x02082220` / `0x020c806c` (96 B, 2x). Retail keeps three arms that assign the same
  constants (h = 0x1e, w = 0x3c), the first one jumping past the other two
  (`blt` to the join), and compares the argument against 0x5a and then 0xb4. Every source
  spelling tried (`if/else if/else`, an explicit `else { if ... }`, the same with `goto`)
  lets 1.2/b56 merge arms two and three and then arm one with them, giving a single
  two-way select in 80 B.
- `ov003 0x020b6b60` / `0x020b732c` (52 B, 2x). Twin no-argument functions that zero six
  halfwords in a 12-byte stack frame and read none of them. Array (`s16 t[6]`, `s16[3][2]`),
  array-of-struct, unrolled stores and unrolled nested loops all collapse to `bx lr` (4 B)
  because the stores are dead; only `volatile` keeps them, and volatile also changes the
  address schedule, so the original source shape is not reachable this way.
- `ov002 0x020bcfe0` / `0x020c2ba8` / `0x020f78c0` (136 B, 3x). Size and every other slot
  match with `s16 a = *(s16 *)(p + 0x66);` reused for the two `func_0203b0c0` calls and the
  third block re-reading the field inline. Only the first block's load schedule is left:
  retail loads `ldrsh a` first, then `[0x90]`, `[0x9c]` and `ldrh [0x8c]` before the `sub`,
  while 1.2/b56 always loads the two words first, `ldrsh` third and the halfword after the
  `sub` (same five instructions, same registers, 24 diff lines that never reorder). Swapping
  the `*` operands, scoping `base` inside the block, hoisting the halfword into a local and
  computing the difference into its own variable all leave the order unchanged.
## 2026-11-23 (fourth session): ov002 family reconstructions that stop at scheduling

- `ov002 0x0204f320` / `0x0204f3cc` (172 B, 2x). Full body recovered: read the id
  byte at `0x36d8`, take `count = table[0x1820 + id*4]`, bail with 0 above `0x40`,
  else store three arguments into `slot[0x1828 + id*0x300 + count*0xc]` and bump the
  count. 1.2/b56 emits 168 B because it CSEs the two id-byte loads that retail keeps
  separate (retail reloads the id after reusing its register for the count address);
  every spelling tried, including separate statements, a `u32 *cnt` pointer and
  computing the row pointer first, stays at 160-168 B.
- `ov002 0x020959a8` / `0x020f6258` (252 B, 2x). Body recovered in full: flag test on
  `slot+0xd8 & 0x80`, `n = *(u16 *)(base + 0x1824 + 0xaa) - 0xc`, a 12-byte `Vec3`
  copy out of `base + 0x1934 + n*0x14 + 4`, field shuffle to `+0x90/+0x94/+0x9c/+0xa0`,
  then the four calls. Naming the three base pointers up front reproduces retail's
  prologue but comes out 256 B instead of 252 B (one extra instruction), and mwcc
  keeps if-converting the early `return 0` into `addeq/moveq/popeq/bxeq` where retail
  branches to a single exit; the `else { r = 0; }` spelling grows it to 260 B.
- `ov002 0x020a5d48` / `0x020c583c` (168 B, 2x). Two-call spawner with a 4-word
  stack block reused between the calls (`0x10000000`, `slot[0]`, `0`, `index`) and a
  `0x34`-stride table indexed by `slot[0]`; the first call's fourth argument is
  `(*(u8 *)(base + index*0x158 + 0x2e6) >> 1) == 0`. Not attempted past the read.
- `ov002 0x0208f38c` and its 12 byte-identical siblings (608 B, 13x, `0x0208f860`
  group) are fully mapped but not reconstructed: `index*0x158` slot, `0x1744 + index*0x38`
  record, `§/5` on `slot[0x128]*4`, `/360` on the shifted y, the 0x200000 direction
  word flipped when the slot type is 1, an eleven-argument `func_ov002_02057068` call,
  then clearing bit 0 of `base[0x38]`.
## 2026-11-23 (fifth session): the 608-byte ov002 screen writer, first reconstruction

- `ov002 0x0208f38c` and 12 byte-identical siblings (608 B, 13x). A full first
  draft now compiles to 572 B (`build/cand/note_f38c_best.c` in the build tree):
  `slot = base + 0x2e4 + i*0x158`, `p = base + 0x1744 + i*0x38`,
  `sb = base + 0x1824`, `t2 = *(u8 *)(base + i*0x158 + 0x2e6) >> 1`,
  `dir = (s8)(-(t2*2 - 1))`, `ang = (func_ov002_0205bf64(i) - 0x14) << 16`,
  `cnt = (*(s32 *)(slot + 0x128) * 4) / 5` (signed field, or the division turns
  into `umull`), `y = (s16)func_ov002_02053468(0x200000)`, the two `+/- dv`
  blocks on `slot[0x4c] & 4` / `slot[0x50] & 0x10|0x20`, the `/360` yaw into
  `func_ov002_0204f770`, the `v = 0x200000` negation on `t2 == 1`, the two
  `ang = -ang` cases, `func_ov002_020532e4`, the `0x157c` area term into
  `func_0203b0c0`, `func_ov002_020528cc`, the eleven-argument
  `func_ov002_02057068`, `func_ov002_020780f8`, then `base[0x38] &= ~1`.
  Residue: retail spills `idx` and `p` to `sp+0x20` / `sp+0x24` and keeps eight
  outgoing argument slots (`sp+0..0x1c`), while the draft keeps them in registers
  and its argument block is one word shorter, so the frame is 0x34 instead of
  0x3c and the prologue schedules differently; retail also emits
  `add r0, r0, #0` after `func_ov002_02053468` where the draft does not.
- `ov002 0x02068c78` (64 B). Three byte stores into `base + 0x1e6c + (slot[2] >> 1)`.
  Reproduces at exactly 64 B with the shift as an unsigned `u32` (a `u8` shift emits
  `asr`, retail has `lsr`); the only residue is the register mirror: retail keeps
  `base` in `ip`, the shifted byte in `r3` and the target pointer in `r1`, 1.2/b56
  keeps them in `r3`, `r2` and `r2`.
- `ov002 0x02081078` (60 B) and `0x0206308c` (56 B). Both reproduce at their retail
  size but with a different first temporary: 0x02081078 wants the `data_ov002_02102070`
  pointer and the slot offset in `r2` and gets them in `r1`/`r0`; 0x0206308c wants
  `0x027c0000` and `0x3ff8` split across two pooled constants read back through a
  pre-indexed `ldr r0, [r1, r0]!`, while every spelling (`*(u32 *)0x027c3ff8`,
  `(u8 *)0x027c0000 + 0x3ff8`, a `u32 *p = 0x027c0000; p[0xffe]`, volatile or not)
  folds the address into one constant and compiles to 48-52 B.
- `ov002 0x0208f38c` (608 B, 13 byte-identical members) -- UPDATE: the draft now
  reaches 604 of 608 bytes with the frame and spill layout identical to retail
  (`idx` at `sp+0x20`, `p` at `sp+0x24`, `v` at `sp+0x28`, the `Vec3` out at
  `sp+0x2c..0x34`, eight outgoing argument slots, frame `0x3c`). Two corrections
  were needed: the screen call `func_ov002_02057068` takes **twelve** arguments
  -- `(res.x, res.y, res.z, 0, 0, 0, t2, 0x8000000, 0xb, *(u8 *)slot,
  *(u16 *)(slot + 0x12c), idx)` -- not eleven (the third literal zero supplies
  the eighth stack slot), and `ang` must be `u32`, not `s32`: retail negates it
  as `mvn r0, #0` + unsigned `mul`, which an `s32` turns into a single `rsb`.
  Residual: retail emits `add r0, r0, #0` after `func_ov002_02053468` (no cast
  spelling tried reproduces it; `(s16)(u16)` gives `lsl`/`lsr` instead), loads
  `v` back from `sp+0x28` for the `t2 == 1` negation (`ldr`/`mvn`/`mul` versus
  the draft's folded `addeq sp` + `rsb`; a `volatile s32 v` gets `ldr`/`rsb` and
  costs four bytes), and schedules `mul r0, r6, fp` before the `/360` yaw
  division where the draft emits it after.
- `ov002 0x0208f38c` (608 B x13) -- UPDATE 2: the draft is now exactly 608 bytes
  with retail's frame and spill offsets. Making `v` a **`u32`** (not `s32`) and
  reading it back through a pointer (`u32 *vp = &v; v = *vp * -1;`) reproduces
  retail's `ldr`/`mvn r1, #0`/`mul` negation, and that closed the last four bytes.
  What is left is schedule and one instruction of shape, not semantics: retail
  spills `idx` to `sp+0x20` right after the `mul` and reloads it before the
  `mla`, defers `lsr r3, r2, #1` until after the `mla` (the draft emits it
  immediately after the `ldrb`), emits `add r0, r0, #0` between
  `func_ov002_02053468` and its `(s16)` conversion, and computes `mul r0, r6, fp`
  before the `/360` yaw division where the draft emits it after. Moving the `dir`
  or `t2` assignment next to its use costs 12 bytes, so the declaration order is
  already right.
- `arm9 0x0202612c` (20 B). `void f(void) { func_02026140(); func_020261d0(); }`.
  Retail is `stmdb sp!, {lr}; bl; bl; ldm sp!, {lr}; bx lr` with **no frame**, but
  mwcc 1.2/b56 reserves `sub sp, sp, #4` for every function that makes two or
  more calls (verified with an isolated two-call probe: one call becomes a tail
  call, two or three calls always get the four-byte frame, regardless of return
  type, parameters or block nesting). The retail shape is therefore not
  reachable from C with this compiler.
- Tiny band, exhaustively re-scanned: only 27 unmatched functions of 0x30 bytes
  or less remain across arm9 and all overlays (an earlier scan that reported 248
  was wrong -- it keyed `src/arm9/<name>.c` for overlay symbols). The remainder is
  BIOS `svc` wrappers, `ldm`/`stm` block-copy loops, ARM/Thumb interworking stubs,
  delinked epilogue fragments and `udf`, i.e. the tiny band is exhausted. Two
  apparent easy wins collapse under compilation: `ov003 0x02050f2c` (16 B,
  `cmp/moveq r0,#0/movne r0,#0`) folds to 8 bytes from every spelling because
  both arms return the same constant.
- `ov009 0x0204f93c` / `0x02051080` (212 B x2, famsurvey group). Overlay display
  setup: an aggregate-initialised three-`u16` block, a 0x30-byte clear, a
  `DISPCNT` field update, one 3-word vector copy from `data_ov009_02054f50`, one
  zeroed vector, one 3-word copy from `data_ov009_02054f44`, then
  `func_02022994(&v0, &v2, &v1, 0)` and two writes to `0x040004c8`/`0x040004cc`.
  Two useful facts fell out: the three `strh` only survive as a *declaration-time*
  aggregate initialiser (`struct { u16 a, b, c; } s = { 0, 0, 0 };` -- separate
  assignments are dead-store eliminated), and the 0x30-byte scratch buffer must be
  a `u32 buf[0xc]` (a `u8 buf[0x30]` is not aligned to 4 and shifts every later
  local by two, so the whole tail lands two bytes off). Best draft is 208 of 212
  bytes: retail keeps the `v0` address in `r5` and reuses it (`add r5, sp, #0x3c`
  ... `mov r0, r5`), materialises the zero struct as `mov lr, #0`, and has one
  extra pool load; the draft uses `r4`, `mov r3, #0` and an `ldr lr, [pc]`. Pure
  register-allocation residue, 52 instructions against retail's 53.
- `ov004 0x020579c0` / `0x02057dcc` (372 B x2, famsurvey group). Scene setup: two
  bss base pointers, `memset(p, 0, 0x10)` with `*(u32 *)(p + 4) = -1`, an early
  `if (func_ov004_02053510(g + 0x178) == 0)` path that pokes `0x0400000a`
  (`(v & 0x43) | 0x18c` then `&= ~0x40`), copies a two-`u16` struct out of
  `data_ov004_02063ac4`, and makes the **fourteen-argument** call

  0, 2, 1, 0, 0, 1, g + 0x178)`; then `func_ov004_020528c0` /
  `func_ov004_020528c8` (the 120 B teardown matched this session), a second

  `func_ov004_020576bc(0x340, func_0200fd40())`, `0x04000000`'s `0x1f00` field set
  to `0x1e00`, `func_ov004_02052464(g + 0x35f0, -0x10, 0, 0x1e)` and `return 1`.

  pokes after the struct copies). The pointer/struct split that *did* matter:
  the copy must be a struct assignment from `*(P2 *)data_ov004_02063ac4`, not two
  field loads.
  Rechecked: eight declaration orders of `p`/`g`/`a`/`b`/`q` all give the same
  three-register rotation (declaration order does move callee-saved colours, but
  not these caller-saved scratch ones), and the underlying residue is the
  scheduler emitting the `a` zero-init before the `b` copy where retail copies
  `b` first; swapping the two statements in the source does not change it.
- `ov009 0x0204f93c` / `0x02051080` (212 B x2) -- UPDATE: moving the zeroed

  `func_02025c94`/`func_02026a50` pair on `base+0x4658`). The single difference is
  the scratch colouring inside the loop: retail materialises the stored zero in
  `lr` (`mov lr, r5`) and the `base + i*2` pointer in `ip` (`add ip, r4, r5,
  lsl #1`), mwcc the reverse. Tried: inline `base + 0x4a58 + i*2` (168->240 B with
  a pool literal), `u8 *p` inside the loop, pointer declared at function scope,
  `u16 *` base, a `u16 z = 0` store value, `flag`-as-zero, `i != 0x200` vs
  `i < 0x200`, the two stores swapped, and expression-order permutations. All
  land at 220 B with the same swap. Same tie as `ov003 0x020d72f8` (164 B x2, one
  source shape, six functions, 1208 B).
- `arm9 0x02028920` / `0x02028958` (56 B x2) -- closest draft `build/cand/b_28920.c`
  is 56 B: the increment `p[0x28] += p[0x34]` and the indirect call
  `fn(obj, p[0x2c], old_f28)` are both right, but retail keeps `obj` in `r4` and
  the `p+0x28` address in `lr` (`add lr, r0, #0x28`, `ldr ip, [lr]`) with a
  `push {r4, lr}` frame, while mwcc keeps `p` in `r3` and spills a stack slot
  (`stmdb sp!, {lr}` + `sub sp, sp, #4`). Tried: `u32 *` parameter with array
  indexing, an explicit `u32 *v = &p[0x28]` (60 B), and both declaration orders.
- `arm9 0x02023df8` / `0x02023e58` (96 B x2) -- closest draft
  `build/cand/b_23df8.c` is 92 B: same branch layout and both calls right, but
  retail hoists `mov ip, #0x6600000` once and emits `add r2, ip, r4` in each arm
  where mwcc folds the immediate into two `add rX, r4, #0x6600000`. Tried the
  inverted condition (fixes the layout), `u32 off = 0x6600000`, `u8 *off`,
  unsigned and pointer-cast spellings of the offset: all 92 B.
- `arm9 0x0200f748` (208 B) -- closest drafts `build/cand/a_f748.c` (236 B) and
  `build/cand/b_f748.c` (220 B) reproduce the table walk (0x14-byte entries at
  0x0203cb6c, `out[++*out] = i + f6` for `i < f8`, then the optional `fa`/`fc`
  appends) but mwcc gives the function a bare `stmdb sp!/sub sp,#4` frame where
  retail keeps `n` in `r4` and the entry pointer in `r5` under
  `push {r4, r5, lr}`, and retail's loop pre-test loads `f8` through the
  `data_0203cb74` literal plus the index register instead of `[r5, #8]`.
- `ov009 0x0204f93c` / `0x02051080` (212 B x2) -- closest draft
  `build/cand/v13_f93c.c` is 208 B. Everything else lines up (frame 0x64, the
  three u16 zeroes at sp+6/sp+8/sp+4 through `q = &hdr[1]`, `u32 buf[0xc]` so the
  0x30-byte fill lands at sp+0xc, `Tri` copies at sp+0x3c/0x48/0x54,
  `func_02022994(&s1, &s3, &s2, 1, 0)` with the 5th argument at [sp]). Retail
  additionally keeps s2's address in r4 across the three zero stores and the
  call (`add r4, sp, #0x48` .. `mov r2, r4`); mwcc sinks the constant zero
  stores below s3's copy, addresses them sp-relative and only materialises
  `add r2, sp, #0x48` at the call. Tried: `z = &s2` with `z->` stores, volatile
  variants, statement reordering -- all 204-208 B.
- `ov002 0x0204f478` / `0x0204f50c` (148 B x2) -- `build/cand/a_f478.c` is 148 B
  with an identical instruction sequence: it is a pure register-assignment tie.
  Retail runs the state pointer in r5, the slot count in r6 and the reloaded
  byte index in r4; mwcc gives those three r4/r5/r6 respectively, i.e. a
  3-cycle, and every instruction that touches them encodes differently. The
  byte at base+0x36d8 is loaded three times in retail (0x18, 0x2c, 0x64);
  writing the expression inline at each use keeps all three loads but pins the
  pointer to r4, while hoisting it into a local (`u8 idx`, declared first or
  last, `u8` or `u32`) makes mwcc CSE the second/third load and the unit
  collapses to 140-144 B.
- `arm9 0x02026d54` (216 B, BIOS-style LZ77-to-u16 decompressor) -- the body is
  fully decoded (Ghidra in project ATOJ9 agrees): `len = *(u32 *)src >> 8`,
  `src += 4`, then per flag byte an 8-bit loop; literal arm `acc |= *src++ << pos;
  len--; pos ^= 8;` with `*dst++ = acc` when `pos` wraps, back-reference arm
  `n = (*src >> 4) + 3; back = (src[1] | ((*src & 0xf) << 8)) + 1; src += 2;`
  then `n` copies of
  `(*(u16 *)((u8 *)dst - ((back + ((8 - pos) >> 3)) & ~1)) & (0xff << sh)) >> sh`
  with `sh = ((8 - pos) ^ ((back & 1) << 3)) ^ 8`. A straight transcription
  compiles, but mwcc needs 16 more bytes and spills to the stack (frame 0xc,
  `fp` used) where retail runs the whole thing in r2-r8/sb/sl with no frame;
  block-scoping and flattening the temporaries both spill. Needs the original
  source's variable structure before it will fit.
- `ov004 0x020509d8` (280 B, SJIS decimal writer) -- `build/cand/e_509d8.c` is
  272 B: digit extraction (`buf[n++] = v % 10; v /= 10;`), the 0xb minus sign,
  the 10/11 sentinels in `buf[0]`, the two-byte table lookup at
  `data_ov004_020636e8` and the trailing NUL are all right. Retail spends two
  extra instructions materialising the constants (`mov r6,#0` then
  `add r5,r6,#1` for the initial `n = 1`, and it keeps `buf[0] = 10` in r4
  across the `v == 0` test) and its frame is 0x84 where mwcc's is 0x88, i.e.
  retail has exactly one local array and no spilled temporaries. Tried
  do-while vs while for the digit loop, `buf[1] = 0; n = 2;` vs `buf[n++] = 0;`,
  `k`/`p` addressing, and both statement orders for the sentinel store.
- `ov004 0x020509d8` again (280 B) -- `s8 *tbl`, `buf[n++] = 10;` for the
  sentinel (that swap of the two register assignments is what retail's
  `mov r6,#0; add r5,r6,#1; mov r4,#0xa; str r4,[sp]` wants) and
  `s8 *q = (s8 *)data_ov004_020636e8 + buf[i] * 2;` inside the write loop
  (so mwcc hoists the table load after the count guard and emits `ldrsb`)
  get the candidate to 280 B with every instruction before `0xb0` identical.
  The residue is the write loop only: retail keeps `i` in ip (scratch) and
  tbl/buf/digit/k in r6/r5/r4/r7, mwcc has `i` in r6 and reorders the
  `ldr r6,[r1]` / `add r5,sp,#0` pair. 16 of 70 instructions still differ;
  declaration order of `i`/`k`, `for` vs `while`, and `k` addressing do not
  move it. Pure allocation/scheduling tie at equal size.
- `ov002 0x02068e2c` (276 B, 9-slice sine-pair writer).  Decoded: base +
  0x1e6c + 0x600 + i*8, four `(k * tab) + 0x800) >> 12` halfword stores per
  step, `tab = data_0203d908` with element indices `2*x` and `2*x+1`, and three
  counters incremented by 0x8000/0x100/0x200.  `k0 = 0x100 + b/8`,
  `k1 = 0x200 + c/8`, and the table index comes from `a/8` truncated to 16 bits
  then shifted right 4 (`lsl #13; lsr #16; asr #4`).  Every candidate that
  reproduces the arithmetic lands at 244-252 B: mwcc zeroes 8 registers where
  retail zeroes 4 and spills the base to the stack instead of keeping it in
  r5 with the counters in lr/ip/r3.  Residue is allocation, not expression.
- `arm9 0x0202612c` (20 B, two back-to-back calls) -- `void f(void) {
  func_02026140(); func_020261d0(); }` compiles to 28 B because mwcc pads the
  frame (`sub sp, sp, #4`) for the call alignment; retail has none.  Not fixed
  by dropping prototypes or returning a value.
- `arm9 0x020263c0` (12 B) -- retail is `subs r0, r0, #4; bhs self; bx lr`,
  i.e. a fused bottom-tested loop.  `while (n >= 4) n -= 4;` gives the test at
  the top, `do { } while` gives an unfolded `sub` plus a separate `cmp`, and a
  void return lets mwcc delete the loop entirely.
- `arm9 0x020048e8` (20 B) -- word fill `add ip, r1, r2; cmp r1, ip;
  stmlt r1!, {r0}; blt`.  The bound is an unscaled `p + n`, so `n` counts bytes
  while the store advances 4, and the compare is signed.  Casting the end
  pointer and comparing as `s32` both still emit `lsl #2`/`blo`.
- `arm9 0x02026aa4` / `0x02026ac8` (36 B / 44 B) -- straight copies built from
  `ldm r0!, {r2,r3,ip}` / `stm r1!, {r2,r3,ip}` pairs (four pairs, then a final
  four-register pair for the 64-byte one).  A `u32` loop gives a 28 B byte loop,
  a plain struct assignment gives 52/60 B with 4-register ldm/stm and a frame,
  and unrolling by hand gives 84 B.  mwcc will not pick the three-register
  transfer without a source reason that is not a struct copy.
- `arm9 0x02024b5c` (48 B) -- `p = (u32 *)0x027fffb0; if (bit >= 0x60) { p++;
  bit -= 0x60; } else bit -= 0x40; *p |= 0x80000000u >> bit;` gives 44 B: retail
  predicates the select (`addpl`/`subpl`/`submi`) and keeps `ldr` after the
  shifts, mwcc uses `addhs`/`subhs`/`sublo` and hoists the load.
- `arm9 0x0203b58c` (16 B) -- `asm { mov r1, #0; mov r0, #0x18;
  swi 0x123456 }` matches every instruction except the return: retail ends
  `mov pc, lr`, mwcc emits `bx lr` (same size).  Its siblings 0x0203b564 /
  0x0203b578 want a saved `lr` and `pop {pc}`, which mwcc does not add for an
  asm-only body either.
- `arm9 0x02029aa0` (140 B) -- decoded as `start = p[0x28]; n = min(a2,
  p[0x24] - start); p[0x2c] = a1; if (n < 0) n = 0; p[0x30] = a2;
  p[0x34] = n; if (a3 == 0) p[0xc] |= 4; func_020291f4(p, 0);` then the
  a3 == 0 branch re-reads `p[0x28]` minus the saved start, or -1.  Every form
  compiles to exactly 140 B; retail homes `start` in r4, `a3` in r5 and `n` in
  r6, mwcc insists on `start` in r5 with `n` in r4.  Declaration order,
  inline vs named `len`, and splitting the initialisers all keep the swap.
- `arm9 0x02033b34` (140 B) -- insert-with-shift-up over 16-byte blocks at
  base+0x10: `if (idx > *base) error(...); if (*base != idx) for (i = *base - 1;
  i >= idx; i--) blk[i + 1] = blk[i]; (*base)++;`.  Note `*base` is read three
  times in retail, so no `n` variable.  Candidate reaches 140 B with the right
  block copy (`ldm/stm` of four words) but homes the index in r6 and top-tests
  the loop, where retail homes it in lr and bottom-tests it, which also drops
  the extra push.  `for` / `while` / `do`-`while` / pre-checked `if` / pointer
  walk all land on the r6 top-tested shape.
- `arm9 0x02033bc0` and `0x02033c0c` (16 B each) are not C functions: each is
  two `mov r0, #imm; b 0x02033bd0` / `b 0x02033c1c` pairs with no test between
  them, i.e. switch-case thunks.  Their targets start with the same veneer
  `ldr r1, =self+8; bx r1` before the real body (`mrs apsr` / mask a bit /
  `msr cpsr_fc` / return the previous state), so the 60-byte symbols at
  0x02033bd0 and 0x02033c1c are two-entry-point locals, not standalone units.
- `ov003 0x020538b0` (320 B, two 4-way field classifier chains).  Decoded: for
  `a0 == 0` test `p[0x200+0x9c]`, `+0xa2`, `+0x9e`, `+0xa0` in that order; for
  `a0 != 0` the same set in the order 0x9c, 0x9e, 0xa0, 0xa2.  Each test is
  `if (v != 0 && v - 1 == p[0x200+0x6c]) return 1;` and the field is reloaded
  per test.  Every formulation lands at exactly 320 B with the right block
  layout and predication, but mwcc homes the loaded base pointer in r0 and the
  `p + 0x200` temporary in r1 where retail uses r2 and r0 (retail's `a0` stays
  in r0 across the dereference, so the load goes to r2).  Declaration order,
  `u16` vs `u32` for `v`, `&&` vs nested `if`, `switch (a0)`, a real
  `q = p + 0x200` variable, a two-argument signature and `const` pointers were
  all tried.  Pure allocation tie: 48 of 80 instructions differ, only by
  register name.
- `ov002 0x02088168` (336 B, pair-marking pass).  Decoded: zero eight bytes at
  `base+0x4bbc`, then `for (i = 0; i < 4; i++) p[i] = 0;`; bail unless
  `base[0x8c] == 1`; `perm[base[0x94 + i]] = i`; then for `i` in 0..1 take
  `a = perm[2i]`, `b = perm[2i+1]`, write `p[a + 4] = b`, `p[b + 4] = a`, and
  mark the pair 1/1 when both `base[0x98 + x]` are 1, else 2/3 per element.
  Exact size reached, but retail folds `base[0x94 + i]` as `add base, i` then
  `ldrb [that, #0x94]` and keeps `p + a` / `p + b` in r2/r1, while mwcc folds
  `add i, #0x94` and rotates the pair pointers to r3/r2.  Casting through a
  header struct fixes the fold but adds an induction `mov`/`add`; `u32 perm[4]`
  instead of `u32 perm[2][2]` costs 12 bytes.  Best candidate
  `build/cand/d_88168.c`, 21 differing register slots out of 84.
- `ov003 0x02054190` (336 B, 2x8 halfword transpose + packed register write).
  Decoded: `h = base + 0x8700; h->idx = 0;` then a three-way select on `a0`
  writing four halfwords at `base + 0x89bc` (0x80/0x80/0x60/0x60 for 1,
  0x80/0x80/0/0xc0 for 7, 0/0xff/0/0xc0 otherwise); then
  `for (i = 0; i < 2; i++) tbl[k][i] = src[k]` for k = 0..7 (an `s16[8][2]`
  transpose, and the k loop is fully unrolled in retail); then read
  `f = tbl + idx` and store `(f[0] << 8 | f[1] & 0xff)` style packs to the
  halfwords at 0x04000040 and 0x04000044 (`f[0]`, `f[2]`, `f[4]`, `f[6]`).
  Writing the eight `tbl[k][i] = src[k]` statements explicitly reproduces the
  unrolled body (the nested `k` loop is not unrolled), giving 344 B against
  336: the four pack operands are addressed as `idx`, `idx + 2`, `idx + 4`,
  `idx + 6` scaled separately, where retail computes `flat + idx` once and
  uses `#4`/`#8`/`#0xc` immediates.  Introducing that pointer variable drops
  the unit to 316 B instead (mwcc then merges the source reads with the
  stores), and `u16 i` costs a further 8 B.  Residue is allocation and
  addressing form, not the algorithm.
- `ov003 0x02052384` (372 B, RGB555 -> BGR555 shade).  Sibling of the matched
  0x02052798/0x0205286c: 17 rows of 16 colours, each channel taken as
  `src[i] & mask` and offset by the row index `j` when the mode `a0` selects
  that channel (red for `a0 == 0 || 3 || 5`, green for `a0 == 1 || k1 <= 1`
  with `k1 = (u16)(a0 - 3)`, blue for `a0 == 2 || k2 <= 1` with
  `k2 = (u16)(a0 - 4)`), then clamped to 0x1f and packed at shifts 0/5/10.
  Exact size, right block layout, right branch polarity and right pooled
  `0xfffd`/`0xfffc` adds, but mwcc keeps the two mode deltas in r8/r7 and the
  loop temporaries in r4/r5/lr where retail uses r4/lr for the deltas and
  r6/r5/r7 for the temporaries.  All 24 declaration orders, `k <= 1` vs
  `k < 2`, `if`/`else` vs `if`/`&&`, `row` via `u16 *` vs `u8 *`, `v` at
  function scope, braced clamps and `s32` for `a0` were tried; every variant
  is instruction-identical and differs only in register names.
- `ov009 0x0204e500` (336 B) and `ov005 0x0205587c` (336 B) are the same source
  with different constants (`base + 0x356`/`0x5d4` vs `base + 0x438e`/`0x460c`,
  different global and pool addresses).  Decoded: clear the u16 at
  `base + 0x356`, set four halfwords at `w + 0x20` by mode (1: 80/80/60/60,
  7: 80/80/0/c0, else 0/ff/0/c0), then `for (i = 0; i < 2; i++)` copy
  `w[0x10..0x17]` into `w[i + 2k]` as eight explicit halfword statements, then
  build two halfwords at 0x04000040/0x04000044 from
  `w[idx]`, `w[idx+2]`, `w[idx+4]`, `w[idx+6]` as `((v << 8) & 0xff00) | (v & 0xff)`.
  Exact size with `s16 *` pointers, but mwcc needs no callee-saved registers
  (it parks the zero constant in ip and the pointer in r1, emitting
  `stmdb sp!, {lr}`) where retail pushes `{r4, r5, lr}` and threads r4/r5
  through the copy loop.  All 120 declaration orders of the five locals give
  the same 71-slot register-only difference, under 1.2/b56 and 1.2/sp2 alike.
- `ov003 0x02082220` (96 B, twice: 0x02082220 and 0x020c806c).  Three-way
  select on `a1 < 0x5a` / `< 0xb4` setting `n = 0x3c`, `t = 0x1e`, a
  `if (t == 0) return 0;` guard and `return (s16)func_0203b0c0(n * a1, t);`.
  All three branches set the same constants in retail, so the shape is a
  plain if/else-if/else; the 96 B form keeps a `blt` out of the first branch
  and if-converts the second test into a `movlt`/`movge` pair.  mwcc folds
  identical branch bodies before block merging, so every spelling that emits
  the right order in all three branches collapses to 80 B, while every
  spelling that survives keeps either the middle pair or the if-converted pair
  in the wrong `movlt r2`/`movlt r3` order.  Constant spellings, casts,
  reordered assignments and both declaration orders were tried.
- `ov003 0x020b6b60` (52 B, twice).  Zeroes three 2x`u16` stack pairs via base
  pointers (`r1 = sp`, `r2 = sp + 4`) rather than direct `sp`-relative stores.
  The stores survive only as `volatile` locals (plain locals are dead-store
  eliminated to a 4-byte unit), and `volatile` removes the base-pointer temps:
  every shape tried is 40 B with `strh r0, [sp, #n]`.  An inlined
  `static void clr(volatile u16 *p)` is not inlined (mwcc emits `bl`).
- `arm9 0x02023df8` and `0x02023e58` (96 B each, identical but for the
  `0x6600000` vs `0x6400000` bias) look like trivial tail wrappers: load
  `data_02042370`, and unless it is `0xffffffff` call `func(that, a0, bias + a1, a2)`
  and otherwise call `func2(a0, bias + a1, a2)`.  They cannot be written in C
  today: both `bl` targets, 0x02068e0 and 0x02069ec, have no symbol in any
  module - they are past the end of arm9 (`.text` ends 0x0203b6e8, `.bss` at
  0x0204c7c0, and no symbol in `config/ATOJ/arm9/symbols.txt` is above
  0x0204c798) and land inside overlay 3, whose `.text` starts exactly at
  0x0204c7c0.  So arm9 calls into ov003 at fixed addresses that are not function
  starts, and an `extern` declaration cannot resolve.  Any attempt fails to
  compile with a bare `<string not found>` on the call line.
- `ov003 0x020524f8` (340 B, 16-pixel BGR555 halving loop) is 340 B with only 39
  differing slots, and every one is the r4/r5 role swap: `col` lands in r5 and
  `k` in r4 from the second channel on, where retail keeps `col` in r4 and `k`
  in r5 (the first channel is byte-exact).  The shape that reproduces the rest is
  `col = (u16)((src[i] & 0x3e0) >> 5); n = col; n >>= 1; for (k = 0; k < n; k++)
  { if (col != 0) col--; } if (col > 0x1f) col = 0x1f; dst[i] += col << 5;` -
  note `n = col; n >>= 1;` is required for `lsr` instead of `asr`.  All 24
  declaration orders of `i`/`col`/`n`/`k` give 39 diffs, per-channel variables
  (`col1`..`k3`) are worse (348 B, 62-76 diffs), and `u16 t = src[i]` /
  `col = src[i]` pre-load forms do not move it either.
- `ov002 0x020604b4` (352 B) compiles to exactly 352 B with 19 differing slots, all
  of them the r4/r5/lr roles inside the two plain "store 4"/"store 3" loops: retail
  keeps `e` (entry pointer) in `lr`, `pp` in `r5` and `i` in `r4`, while mwcc gives
  `e` r5, `pp` r4 and `i` lr.  The three-channel dispatch itself is matched, and so
  is the first loop (`bne` to the cold `store 3` block, `poplo` for the unsigned
  `mode < 2` test, `addne ip, r1, r5` for `base[i + 0x14a]`).  What reproduces it:
  `u32 mode = base[0x100]; p = base + 0x1824; if (mode < 2) return; idx = mode - 2;
  t = (u32 *)(p + idx * 4); sel = (*(u32 *)(p + 0xb8) & 2) ? 1 : 0;` then
  `if (t[0xe8/4] == 0) { ...main...; return; } else-the-tail-loop`, with `pp`
  declared last, `((u32)e[2] >> 1)` for the `lsr`, and `b = base + i` before the
  two byte stores.  Tried and no better: block-scoped `e`/`pp`, per-loop `i2`,
  index-based entry addressing (`e = base + 0x2e4 + i * 0x158`, 360-364 B),
  `while`/pre-initialised `for`, and six declaration orders (19-32 diffs).
- `ov002 0x0205940c` (384 B, three flag updates on the battle-entry tables) already
  compiles to exactly 384 B with 65 differing slots, all from one register shift:
  retail keeps `base` in r0, `p = base + 0x1824` in lr and `q = base + 0x36ac` in
  r3, while mwcc puts base in r1, p in r0 and q in lr.  Every instruction kind,
  branch, and the `((u32)e[2] >> 1) & 0xff` form (needed for `lsr` + `and #0xff`;
  `(u8)((u32)e[2] >> 1)` makes mwcc fuse it into `cmp r, r, lsr #1`) is right.
  Ten declaration orders, declare-then-assign, and hoisting the `base + 0x2e4`
  pointer to the top all stay at 65.
- `ov003 0x020c7ac0` (376 B, battle-entry init; the sibling of the ov005
  0x0205a670 initialiser) transcribes to 380 B with 76-86 differing slots.  The
  instruction sequence is right from the first entry-table loop on; the missing
  4 bytes are the second pool constant (`0xc8dc`), because retail reuses the
  `0xb8dc` constant already in r2 for `add r3, ip, r2` and only then loads
  `0xc8dc`, while mwcc reloads.  Retail also keeps `base` in r12 (`ldr ip, [r2]`),
  which no declaration order reproduces - all six permutations of `p1`/`p2`/`p3`
  stay at 380 B (76-89 diffs).  Transcribing with inline offsets instead of
  pointer variables spills to the stack (380 B, 94 diffs).

=== RESOLVED arm9 0x0201fcc8, 0x0201fce4, 0x0201fd00: NitroSDK asm, not a 2.0 build ===

All three are hand-written asm blocks in NitroSDK `fx_mtx33.c`
(`MTX_RotX33_`/`MTX_RotY33_`/`MTX_RotZ33_`), assembled by mwasmarm, so no C
shape reproduces them. Installed as `asm void` blocks and assembled by 1.2/b56
byte for byte; the 2.0/base override was retired. See
notes/mwccarm-codegen-log.md, 2026-11-24.

arm9 0x02024a54 func_02024a54
22-channel handler table init; retail keeps table base in lr, mwcc picks r5/r4: 144B, 19 slots, optimize_for_size is a no-op

arm9 0x02036c18 func_02036c18

arm9 0x02036934 func_02036934
740 itcm origin: 0x02036a60-0x02037050 is byte-identical to the itcm section, so this 740 B unit and its three neighbours are the precondition for the eleven itcm units.

Decoded (2026-11-24): soft-float `ldexp` - `double x` in r0:r1, `int n` in r2,
returns `x * 2^n`. It classifies the operand, pre-scales subnormals by 2^54
(`0x4350000000000000`), adds `n` to the exponent, reassembles the high word
with `0x800fffff`, overflows via `1e300 * copysign(1e300, x)` and underflows via
two `1e-300` multiplies. The exact constant list and the full body are in
notes/mwccarm-codegen-log.md (2026-11-24).

Best shape is 740/740 B with the same instruction multiset: the parameter is an
8-byte `struct { double d; struct { u32 lo, hi; } w; }` passed by value, the
body works through `w` and the local copy. 123-128 slots differ because retail
loads the aggregate from its home slot into r5/r4 and keeps working on that home
at [sp+0x18] (mwcc works on the local at [sp] with lo in r1), and retail lays
the exp==0 body out of line behind `beq` where mwcc puts it inline behind `bne`.
Fifteen spellings tried (double vs aggregate parameter, declaration order, dead
copy, `!= 0` first test, conditional expressions). Parked as a
register/layout wall.

residue is the table store: retail keeps the store address live across the
`str r4, [r1, r6, lsl #2]!` write-back so the same register becomes the second
argument of the `func_01ff8484` copy, and its argument homes sit one register
higher (r6/r5/r4/r8/r7 vs r7/r6/r5/r4/r8). Best candidate 204 B. Tried the
pointer-local form, the `&dst[a]` call argument and the direct `dst[a]` store.
